  label: 5. Taddhitakaṇḍa
---

import { LinkCard } from "@astrojs/starlight/components";

:::note
The `taddhita` suffixes are grouped by the sense they express: descent (`apacca`), relation and the many other senses of `ṇika` and its kin, the abstract, comparison, possession, number and the adverbial suffixes. Each derivation shows the vuddhi, the elision of the marker `ṇ` and the sandhi that the Kaccāyana rules prescribe.

:::

:::note[The seven kinds of taddhita]
Buddhappiya groups the secondary suffixes by sense, in Kaccāyana's order, and brings the operations they share forward to the first derivation that needs them. The ranges are Rūpasiddhi numbers.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  T(["Taddhitakaṇḍa 361–423"]) --> A["Apaccataddhita 361–372<br/>descent: ṇa, ṇāyana, ṇāna, ṇeyya, ṇi, ṇava, ṇera"]
  T --> R["Saṃsaṭṭhādianekatthataddhita 373–386<br/>relation and other senses: ṇika, ṇa, ima, iya, kaṇ, tā, la, ālu"]
  T --> Bh["Bhāvataddhita 387–389<br/>the abstract: ṇya, tta, tā, ṇa, kaṇ"]
  T --> V["Visesataddhita 390–397<br/>comparison: tara, tama, isika, iya, iṭṭha"]
  T --> P["Assatthitaddhita 398–405<br/>possession: vī, sī, ika, ī, ra, vantu, mantu, ṇa; maya"]
  T --> N["Saṅkhyātaddhita 406–418<br/>ordinals and the higher numbers"]
  T --> Ab["Abyayataddhita 419–423<br/>adverbs: dhā, thā, thaṃ; the general operations"]
```

:::

## Apaccataddhita (Suffixes of descent)

> Atha nāmato eva vibhatyantā apaccādiatthavisese taddhituppattīti nāmato paraṃ taddhitavidhānamārabhīyate.

Now, because a `taddhita` (secondary derivative) arises only from a noun — from a lemma that carries an inflection — in a particular sense such as "offspring", the treatment of the `taddhita` is begun after that of the noun [and its compounds].

> Tattha tasmā tividhaliṅgato paraṃ hutvā hitā sahitāti taddhitā, ṇā dipaccayānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ, tesaṃ vā nāmikānaṃ hitā upakārā taddhitāti anvatthabhūtaparasamaññāvasenāpi ṇā dippaccayāva taddhitā nāma.

Here `taddhita` means "placed (`hita`), that is, joined on, after (`paraṃ hutvā`) that (`tasmā`) lemma of three genders"; it is a name for the suffixes `ṇa` and the rest. Or else they are `taddhita` because they are of service (`hitā`), helpful, to those (`tesaṃ`) nominal lemmas. So even by way of the terminology of the other grammars, which here says what it means (`anvattha`), it is precisely the suffixes `ṇa` and the rest that are called `taddhita`.

> ‘‘Vasiṭṭhassa apacca’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis (`viggaha`) "Vasiṭṭha's offspring" —

> ‘‘Liṅgañca nipaccate’’ti ito liṅga ggahaṇamanuvattate.

the word `liṅga` (lemma) continues [into the rule] from [61 (§53): Liṅgañca nipaccate](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda/1-pullinga#r61), Kaccāyana's [§53 (61): Liṅgañca nippajjate](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/1-pathamakanda#m53).

---

<a name="r361"></a>

### 361 (§344): Vā ṇa’pacce (Optionally `ṇa` in the sense of offspring) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§344 (361): Vāṇa’pacce" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m344" description="Optionally `ṇa` in the sense of offspring" />
:::

> Chaṭṭhiyantato liṅgamhā ṇa ppaccayo hoti vikappena ‘‘tassa apacca’’miccetasmiṃ atthe.

After a lemma ending in the sixth form ⑥, the suffix `ṇa` is used optionally (`vikappena`) in the sense "his offspring" (`tassa apaccaṃ`).

> So ca –

And that [suffix] —

---

<a name="r362"></a>

### 362 (§432): Dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā (suffixes come after roots and lemmas: a governing statement) :badge[sīhagatika]{variant=caution}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§432 (362): Dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā" href="/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa/2-dutiyakanda#m432" description="suffixes come after roots and lemmas: a governing statement" />
:::

> Dhātūhi, liṅgehi ca paccayā parāva hontīti paribhāsato apaccatthasambandhiliṅgato chaṭṭhiyantāyeva paro hoti. Paṭicca etasmā attho etīti paccayo, patīyanti anena atthāti vā paccayo, ‘‘vuttatthānamappayogo’’ti apacca saddassa appayogo, ‘‘tesaṃ vibhattiyo lopā ce’’ti vibhattilopo ca.

By the metarule ([`paribhāsā`](/introduction#types-of-suttas)) "suffixes come only after roots (`dhātu`) and after lemmas (`liṅga`)", the suffix comes only after the lemma that is connected with the sense of offspring and ends in the sixth form ⑥. It is called a suffix (`paccaya`) because the meaning comes (`eti`) from it, depending on it (`paṭicca`), or because meanings are understood (`patīyanti`) by means of it. The word `apacca` is not used, by [the maxim] "what has already been expressed is not used" (`vuttatthānaṃ appayogo`), and the inflection is elided by [§317 (332): Tesaṃ vibhattiyo lopā ca](/kaccayana/4-samasakappa#m317).

---

<a name="r363"></a>

### 363 (§396): Tesaṃ ṇo lopaṃ (the `ṇ` of these suffixes is elided) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§396 (363): Tesaṃ ṇo lopaṃ" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m396" description="the `ṇ` of these suffixes is elided" />
:::

> Tesaṃ taddhitappaccayānaṃ ṇā nubandhānaṃ ṇa kāro lopamāpajjate.

The letter `ṇ` of these `taddhita` suffixes that carry the marker `ṇ` (`ṇānubandha`) is elided.

---

<a name="r364"></a>

### 364 (§400): Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe ca (optionally `vuddhi` of the first vowel not before a conjunct, before a `ṇ` suffix) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§400 (364): Vuddhādisarassa vā’saṃyogantassa saṇe ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m400" description="optionally `vuddhi` of the first vowel not before a conjunct, before a `ṇ` suffix" />
:::

> Ādisarassa vā ādibyañjanassa vā asaṃyogantassa vuddhi hoti saṇa kārappaccaye pare. Saṃyogo anto assāti saṃyoganto, tadañño asaṃyoganto. Atha vā vavatthitavibhāsatthoyaṃ vā saddo, tadā ca pakatibhūte liṅge sarānamādisarassa asaṃyogantassa vuddhi hoti saṇa kāre taddhitappaccaye pareti attho.

When a suffix with the letter `ṇ` follows, the initial vowel, or [the vowel of] the initial consonant, is strengthened (`vuddhi`), provided it is not followed by a conjunct: what has a conjunct (`saṃyoga`) after it is `saṃyoganta`, "ending in a conjunct", and what is other than that is `asaṃyoganta`. Or else this word `vā` has the sense of a restricted option (`vavatthitavibhāsā`), and then the meaning is that, among the vowels of the lemma that is the base (`pakati`), the initial vowel not followed by a conjunct is strengthened when a `taddhita` suffix with the letter `ṇ` follows.

> Tena –

Hence —

> Vāsiṭṭhādīsu niccāyaṃ, aniccoḷumpikādisu; \
> Na vuddhi nīlapītādo, vavatthitavibhāsato.

In `vāsiṭṭha` ("Vasiṭṭha's offspring") and the like this [strengthening] is invariable (`nicca`); in `oḷumpika` ("one who crosses by raft") and the like it is optional (`anicca`); \
in `nīla` ("dyed blue"), `pīta` ("dyed yellow") and the like there is no strengthening — by the restricted option.

> Ca saddaggahaṇamavadhāraṇatthaṃ.

The mention of the word `ca` is for restriction (`avadhāraṇa`).

> Tassā vuddhiyā aniyamappasaṅge niyamatthaṃ paribhāsamāha.

Since that strengthening would otherwise apply without restriction, a metarule ([`paribhāsā`](/introduction#types-of-suttas)) is stated to restrict it:

---

<a name="r365"></a>

### 365 (§405): Ayuvaṇṇānañcāyo vuddhī (the `vuddhi` of `a`, `i`, `u` is `ā`, `e`, `o`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§405 (365): Ayuvaṇṇānañcāyo vuddhi" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m405" description="the `vuddhi` of `a`, `i`, `u` is `ā`, `e`, `o`" />
:::

> Tesaṃ a kārai vaṇṇu vaṇṇānameva yathākkamaṃ ā eo iccete vuddhiyo honti. Ca saddaggahaṇamavuddhisampiṇḍanatthaṃ, avadhāraṇatthaṃ vā. I ca u ca yu, yu eva vaṇṇā yuvaṇṇā, a ca yuvaṇṇā ca ayuvaṇṇā. Ā ca e ca o ca āyo. Puna vuddhi ggahaṇaṃ ‘‘negamajānapadā’’tiādīsu uttarapadavuddhibhāvatthaṃ, ettha ca ‘‘ayuvaṇṇāna’’nti ṭhānaniyamavacanaṃ āyonaṃ vuddhibhāvappasaṅganivattanatthaṃ.

The strengthened forms (`vuddhi`) of those — of the letter `a`, the `i`-sound and the `u`-sound, and of those alone — are `ā`, `e` and `o` respectively. The mention of the word `ca` is either to include (`sampiṇḍana`) the absence of strengthening or for delimitation. `i` and `u` are `yu`; the sounds that are just `yu` are the `yuvaṇṇā`; `a` together with the `yuvaṇṇā` are the `ayuvaṇṇā`; and `ā`, `e` and `o` are `āyo`. `vuddhi` is mentioned again so that the last member (`uttarapada`) is strengthened in `negamajānapadā` ("townspeople and country people") and the like; and here `ayuvaṇṇānaṃ`, the statement that restricts the locus (`ṭhānaniyama`), is there to stop (`nivattana`) `ā`, `e` and `o` from being liable to strengthening.

> Yathā hi katavuddhīnaṃ, puna vuddhi na hotiha; \
> Tathā sabhāvavuddhīnaṃ, āyonaṃ puna vuddhi na.

For just as vowels already strengthened receive no second strengthening here, \
so `ā`, `e` and `o`, which are strengthened by nature, receive no further strengthening.

> Tato a kārassa ā kāro vuddhi, ‘‘saralopomādesappaccayādimhi saralope tu pakatī’’ti saralopapakatibhāvā, ‘‘naye paraṃ yutte’’ti paranayanaṃ katvā taddhitattā ‘‘taddhitasamāsa’’iccādinā nāmabyapadese kate pure viya syā dyuppatti.

Then the letter `ā` is the strengthening of the letter `a`; the vowel is elided and [the following vowel] keeps its natural form by [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/1-pathamakanda#m83); the [consonant] is joined on to what follows by [§11 (14): Naye paraṃ yutte](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m11); and since the word is a `taddhita`, once it has been designated a noun by [§601 (334): Taddhitasamāsakitakā nāmaṃ vā’ tave tunādīsu ca](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa/4-catutthakanda#m601), the inflections `si` and the rest arise as before.

> Vāsiṭṭho, vasiṭṭhassa putto vā, vāsiṭṭhā iccādi purisa saddasamaṃ. Tassa nattupanattādayopi tadupacārato vāsiṭṭhāyeva. Evaṃ sabbattha gottataddhite paṭhamappakatitoyeva paccayo hoti.

`vāsiṭṭho` ("Vasiṭṭha's offspring"), or `vasiṭṭhassa putto` ("Vasiṭṭha's son"); `vāsiṭṭhā` and so on, the same as the word `purisa`. His grandsons, great-grandsons and the rest are also just `vāsiṭṭhā`, by figurative use (`upacāra`) of that [name]. So in a clan derivative (`gottataddhita`) the suffix is everywhere used after the first base only.

> Itthiyaṃ ṇappaccayantattā ‘‘ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntūhī’’ti vāsiṭṭha saddato īpaccayo. Saralopādiṃ katvā itthippaccayantattā ‘‘taddhitasamāsa’’iccādisutte ca ggahaṇena nāmabyapadese kate syā dyuppatti, vāsiṭṭhī kaññā, vāsiṭṭhī, vāsiṭṭhiyo iccādi itthi saddasamaṃ.

In the feminine, because the word ends in the suffix `ṇa`, the suffix `ī` is used after the word `vāsiṭṭha` by [§239 (190): Ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntuhi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/4-catutthakanda#m239). After the vowel elision and the rest, since the word ends in a feminine suffix, it is designated a noun by the mention of `ca` in the sutta beginning “`Taddhitasamāsa`” [§601], and the inflections `si` and the rest arise: `vāsiṭṭhī kaññā` ("a girl of Vasiṭṭha's line"); `vāsiṭṭhī`, `vāsiṭṭhiyo` and so on, the same as the word `itthī`.

> Napuṃsake vāsiṭṭhaṃ apaccaṃ, vāsiṭṭhāni apaccāni iccādi citta saddasamaṃ. Evaṃ uparipi taddhitantassa tiliṅgatā veditabbā.

In the neuter, `vāsiṭṭhaṃ apaccaṃ` ("Vasiṭṭha's offspring"), `vāsiṭṭhāni apaccāni` and so on, the same as the word `citta`. In the same way, further on too, a word ending in a `taddhita` suffix should be understood to have all three genders (`tiliṅgatā`).

> Bhāradvājassa putto bhāradvājo, vesāmittassa putto vesāmitto, gotamassa putto gotamo. Ettha ca ayuvaṇṇattābhāvā ā kārādīnaṃ na vuddhi hoti. Vasudevassa apaccaṃ vāsudevo, bāladevo[^1]. ‘‘Cittako’’tiādīsu pana saṃyogantattā vuddhi na bhavati.

Bhāradvāja's son is `bhāradvājo`, Vesāmitta's son `vesāmitto` and Gotama's son `gotamo`; here `ā` and the rest are not strengthened, because they are not `a`, `i` or `u`. Vasudeva's offspring is `vāsudevo`; `bāladevo` ("Baladeva's offspring"). In `cittako` ("Cittaka's offspring") and the like, however, there is no strengthening, because [the first vowel] is followed by a conjunct.

> ‘‘Vacchassa apacca’’nti viggahe ṇa mhi sampatte ‘‘vā ṇa’pacce’’ti ito vā ti taddhitavidhāne sabbattha vattate, tena sabbattha vākyavuttiyo bhavanti. ‘‘Apacce’’ti padaṃ yāva saṃsaṭṭha ggahaṇāva vattate.

In the analysis "Vaccha's offspring", when `ṇa` would apply, “`vā`” continues from [361 (§344): Vā ṇa’pacce](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r361) everywhere in the treatment of the `taddhita`; hence everywhere both the phrase (`vākya`) and the derivative (`vutti`) occur. The word `apacce` continues only as far as the mention of `saṃsaṭṭha`.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | vasiṭṭha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vasiṭṭha + ~~sa~~ + ṇa          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | vasiṭṭha + (~~ṇ~~a)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (a→ā) + siṭṭha + a          | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | vāsiṭṭh + (~~a~~ + a)           | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha                        | 14 (§11)  |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha + si (a noun)          | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **vāsiṭṭho**                    |      |

|     | vāsiṭṭha (🚺) + ī               | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vāsiṭṭh + (~~a~~ + ī)           | 190 (§239), 67 (§83) |
| →   | vāsiṭṭhī + si (a noun)          | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vāsiṭṭhī + ~~si~~               | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **vāsiṭṭhī**                    |      |

|     | vāsiṭṭha (🚻)                   | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha + (si→aṃ)              | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **vāsiṭṭhaṃ**                   |      |

:::

[^1]: CST4 prints `baladevo`; `bāladevo` is read with Kaccāyana's vutti under §344.

<a name="r366"></a>

### 366 (§345): Ṇāyana ṇāna vacchādito (`ṇāyana`, `ṇāna` after `vaccha` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§345 (366): Ṇāyana ṇāna vacchādito" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m345" description="`ṇāyana`, `ṇāna` after `vaccha` and the like" />
:::

> Vaccha kacca iccevamādito chaṭṭhiyantato gottagaṇato ṇāyana ṇāna iccete[^2] paccayā honti vā ‘‘tassāpacca’’miccetasmiṃ atthe. Sabbattha ṇa kārānubandho vuddhattho, sesaṃ pubbasamaṃ, saṃyogantattā vuddhiabhāvova viseso.

After the group of clan names (`gottagaṇa`) `vaccha`, `kacca` and the like, ending in the sixth form ⑥, the suffixes `ṇāyana` and `ṇāna` are optionally used in the sense "his offspring". Everywhere the marker `ṇ` is there for the strengthening; the rest is the same as before, and the only difference is that there is no strengthening, because [the first vowel] is followed by a conjunct.

> Vacchāyano vacchāno vacchassa putto vā. Evaṃ kaccassa putto kaccāyano kaccāno, moggallassa putto moggallāyano moggallāno. Evaṃ aggivessāyano aggivessāno, kaṇhāyano kaṇhāno, sākaṭāyano sākaṭāno, muñcāyano muñcāno, kuñjāyano kuñjāno iccādi. Ākatigaṇo’yaṃ.

`vacchāyano`, `vacchāno` ("Vaccha's offspring"), or `vacchassa putto` ("Vaccha's son"). Likewise Kacca's son is `kaccāyano`, `kaccāno`, and Moggalla's son `moggallāyano`, `moggallāno`; likewise `aggivessāyano`, `aggivessāno` ("Aggivessa's son"); `kaṇhāyano`, `kaṇhāno` ("Kaṇha's son"); `sākaṭāyano`, `sākaṭāno` ("Sakaṭa's son"); `muñcāyano`, `muñcāno` ("Muñca's son"); `kuñjāyano`, `kuñjāno` ("Kuñja's son"), and so on. This is a group defined by form (`ākatigaṇa`).

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | vaccha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇāyana       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vaccha + ~~sa~~ + ṇāyana        | 332 (§317) |
| →   | vaccha + (~~ṇ~~āyana)           | 363 (§396) |
| →   | vacch + (~~a~~ + āyana)         | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | vacchāyana + si                 | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vacchāyana + (si→o)             | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **vacchāyano**                  |      |

|     | sakaṭa + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇāna         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakaṭa + ~~sa~~ + ṇāna          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | sakaṭa + (~~ṇ~~āna)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | s + (a→ā) + kaṭa + āna          | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | sākaṭ + (~~a~~ + āna)           | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | sākaṭāna + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | sākaṭāna + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **sākaṭāno**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Kattikāya apacca’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis "Kattikā's offspring" —

[^2]: CST4 prints `iccate`; the form Buddhappiya uses everywhere else is `iccete`.

<a name="r367"></a>

### 367 (§346): Ṇeyyo kattikādīhi (`ṇeyya` after `kattikā` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§346 (367): Ṇeyyo kattikādīhi" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m346" description="`ṇeyya` after `kattikā` and the like" />
:::

> Ādi saddoyaṃ pakāre vattate. Kattikā vinatārohiṇī iccevamādīhi itthiyaṃ vattamānehi liṅgehi ṇeyya ppaccayo hoti vā ‘‘tassāpacca’’miccetasmiṃ atthe.

This word `ādi` occurs in the sense of "kind" (`pakāra`). After lemmas occurring in the feminine, of the kind of `kattikā`, `vinatā` and `rohiṇī`, the suffix `ṇeyya` is optionally used in the sense "his offspring".

> Vibhattilope ‘‘pakati cassa sarantassā’’ti pakatibhāvo. Kattikeyyo, kattikāya putto vā. Evaṃ vinatāya apaccaṃ venateyyo, i kārasse kāro vuddhi, rohiṇiyā putto rohiṇeyyo, gaṅgāya apaccaṃ gaṅgeyyo, bhaginiyā putto bhāgineyyo, nadiyāputto nādeyyo. Evaṃ anteyyo, āheyyo, kāmeyyo. Suciyā apaccaṃ soceyyo, ettha u kārasso kāro vuddhi, bālāya apaccaṃ bāleyyo iccādi.

When the inflection is elided, the lemma keeps its natural form (`pakatibhāva`) by [§318 (333): Pakati cassa sarantassa](/kaccayana/4-samasakappa#m318): `kattikeyyo` ("Kattikā's offspring"), or `kattikāya putto` ("Kattikā's son"). Likewise Vinatā's offspring is `venateyyo`, where the letter `e` is the strengthening of the letter `i`; Rohiṇī's son is `rohiṇeyyo`, Gaṅgā's offspring `gaṅgeyyo`, a sister's (`bhaginī`) son `bhāgineyyo` and a river's (`nadī`) son `nādeyyo`; likewise `anteyyo`, `āheyyo`, `kāmeyyo`. Suci's offspring is `soceyyo`, where the letter `o` is the strengthening of the letter `u`; Bālā's offspring is `bāleyyo`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | kattikā + āya (⑥⨀) + ṇeyya      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kattikā + ~~āya~~ + ṇeyya       | 332 (§317) |
| →   | kattikā + ṇeyya                 | 333 (§318) |
| →   | kattikā + (~~ṇ~~eyya)           | 363 (§396) |
| →   | kattik + (~~ā~~ + eyya)         | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | kattikeyya + si                 | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kattikeyya + (si→o)             | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **kattikeyyo**                  |      |

|     | vinatā + āya (⑥⨀) + ṇeyya       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vinatā + ~~āya~~ + ṇeyya        | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | vinatā + (~~ṇ~~eyya)            | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (i→e) + natā + eyya         | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | venat + (~~ā~~ + eyya)          | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | venateyya + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | venateyya + (si→o)              | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **venateyyo**                   |      |

|     | suci + yā (⑥⨀) + ṇeyya          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | suci + ~~yā~~ + ṇeyya           | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | suci + (~~ṇ~~eyya)              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | s + (u→o) + ci + eyya           | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | soc + (~~i~~ + eyya)            | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | soceyya + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | soceyya + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **soceyyo**                     |      |

:::

> ‘‘Dakkhassāpacca’’nti viggahe ṇamhi sampatte –

In the analysis "Dakkha's offspring", when `ṇa` would apply —

---

<a name="r368"></a>

### 368 (§347): Ato ṇi vā (Optionally `ṇi` after a lemma in `a`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§347 (368): Ato ṇi vā" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m347" description="Optionally `ṇi` after a lemma in `a`" />
:::

> A kārantato liṅgamhā ṇi paccayo hoti vā ‘‘tassāpacca’’miccetasmiṃ atthe. Dakkhi, dakkhī, dakkhayo, doṇassa apaccaṃ doṇi. Evaṃ vāsavi, sakyaputti, nāṭaputti, dāsaputti, dāsarathi. Vāruṇi, kaṇḍi, bāladevi, pāvaki, jinadattassa apaccaṃ jenadatti, suddhodani, anuruddhi iccādi.

After a lemma ending in the letter `a`, the suffix `ṇi` is optionally used in the sense "his offspring": `dakkhi` ("Dakkha's offspring"), `dakkhī`, `dakkhayo`; Doṇa's offspring is `doṇi`. Likewise `vāsavi` ("Vāsava's offspring"), `sakyaputti` ("Sakyaputta's offspring"), `nāṭaputti` ("Nāṭaputta's offspring"), `dāsaputti` ("Dāsaputta's offspring"), `dāsarathi` ("Dasaratha's offspring"); `vāruṇi` ("Varuṇa's offspring"), `kaṇḍi` ("Kaṇḍa's offspring"), `bāladevi` ("Baladeva's offspring"), `pāvaki` ("Pāvaka's offspring"); Jinadatta's offspring is `jenadatti`; `suddhodani` ("Suddhodana's offspring"), `anuruddhi` ("Anuruddha's offspring"), and so on.

> Puna vā ggahaṇena apaccatthe ṇika ppaccayo, aditi ādito ṇya ppaccayo ca. Yathā – sakyaputtassa putto sakyaputtiko, ‘‘tesu vuddhī’’tiādinā ka kārassa ya kāro, sakyaputtiyo. Evaṃ nāṭaputtiko, jenadattiko, vimātuyā putto vemātiko.

By the repeated mention of `vā`, the suffix `ṇika` is used in the sense of offspring, and the suffix `ṇya` after `aditi` and the like. For instance, Sakyaputta's son is `sakyaputtiko`, and with the letter `k` replaced by the letter `y` by [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404), `sakyaputtiyo`; likewise `nāṭaputtiko` ("Nāṭaputta's son"), `jenadattiko` ("Jinadatta's son"); a step-mother's (`vimātu`) son is `vemātiko`.

> ‘‘Aditiyā putto’’ti atthe ṇya ppaccayo, vuddhi ca.

In the sense "Aditi's son", the suffix `ṇya` [is used], and the strengthening —

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | dakkha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇi           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dakkha + ~~sa~~ + ṇi            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | dakkha + (~~ṇ~~i)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | dakkh + (~~a~~ + i)             | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | dakkhi + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | dakkhi + ~~si~~                 | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **dakkhi**                      |      |

|     | jinadatta + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇi        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | jinadatta + ~~sa~~ + ṇi         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | jinadatta + (~~ṇ~~i)            | 363 (§396) |
| →   | j + (i→e) + nadatta + i         | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | jenadatt + (~~a~~ + i)          | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | jenadatti + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | jenadatti + ~~si~~              | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **jenadatti**                   |      |

|     | sakyaputta + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇika     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakyaputta + ~~sa~~ + ṇika      | 332 (§317) |
| →   | sakyaputta + (~~ṇ~~ika)         | 363 (§396) |
| →   | sakyaputt + (~~a~~ + ika)       | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | sakyaputtika + si               | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | sakyaputtika + (si→o)           | 66 (§104) |
| →   | sakyaputti + (k→y) + o          | 370 (§404) (optional) |
| →   | **sakyaputtiko**, **sakyaputtiyo** |      |

|     | vimātu + yā (⑥⨀) + ṇika         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vimātu + ~~yā~~ + ṇika          | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | vimātu + (~~ṇ~~ika)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (i→e) + mātu + ika          | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | vemāt + (~~u~~ + ika)           | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | vemātika + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vemātika + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **vemātiko**                    |      |

:::

---

<a name="r369"></a>

### 369 (§261): Avaṇṇo ye lopañca (`a`/`ā` elided before the suffix `ya`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§261 (369): Avaṇṇo ye lopañca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m261" description="`a`/`ā` elided before the suffix `ya`" />
:::

> A vaṇṇo taddhitabhūte ya ppaccaye pare lopamāpajjate. Ca saddena ivaṇṇopīti i kāralopo, ‘‘yavataṃ talanadakārānaṃ byañjanāni ca la ña jakāratta’’nti tya kārasaṃyogassa ca kāro, ‘‘paradvebhāvo ṭhāne’’ti dvittaṃ, ādicco. Evaṃ ditiyā putto decco.

The `a`-vowel is elided when the `taddhita` suffix `ya` follows, and by the word `ca` the `i`-vowel too. Hence the letter `i` is elided, the conjunct `ty` becomes the letter `c` by [§269 (41): Yavataṃ ta la ṇa dakārānaṃ byañjanāni ca la ña jakārattaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m269), and it is doubled (`dvitta`) by [§28 (40): Para dvebhāvo ṭhāne](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m28): `ādicco` ("Aditi's son"). Likewise Diti's son is `decco`.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | aditi + yā (⑥⨀) + ṇya           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | aditi + ~~yā~~ + ṇya            | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | aditi + (~~ṇ~~ya)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | (a→ā) + diti + ya               | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | ādit + (~~i~~ + ya)             | 369 (§261) |
| →   | ādi + (ty→c) + a                | 41 (§269) |
| →   | ādi + (c→cc) + a                | 40 (§28)  |
| →   | ādicca + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | ādicca + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **ādicco**                      |      |

|     | diti + yā (⑥⨀) + ṇya            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | diti + ~~yā~~ + ṇya             | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | diti + (~~ṇ~~ya)                | 363 (§396) |
| →   | d + (i→e) + ti + ya             | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | det + (~~i~~ + ya)              | 369 (§261) |
| →   | de + (ty→c) + a                 | 41 (§269) |
| →   | de + (c→cc) + a                 | 40 (§28)  |
| →   | decca + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | decca + (si→o)                  | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **decco**                       |      |

:::

> ‘‘Kuṇḍaniyā putto’’ti atthe ṇya ppaccaye kate –

When the suffix `ṇya` has been added in the sense "Kuṇḍanī's son" —

> ‘‘Kvacādimajjhuttaresū’’ti vattate.

“`Kvacādimajjhuttaresu`” ("in some places, in the first, middle and last [positions]") continues from [§403 (354): Kvacādimajjhuttarānaṃ dīgharassā paccayesu ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m403).

---

<a name="r370"></a>

### 370 (§404): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca (and in these, `vuddhi`, elision, insertion, modification, metathesis, replacement) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404" description="and in these, `vuddhi`, elision, insertion, modification, metathesis, replacement" />
:::

> Tesu ādimajjhuttaresu avihitalakkhaṇesu jinavacanānuparodhena kvaci vuddhi lopa āgama vikāra viparītaādesā hontīti saṃyogantattepi ādivuddhi, i kāralope nya ssañā deso, koṇḍañño, kuruno putto korabyo, etthāpi teneva u kārassa avā deso, bhātuno putto bhātabyo.

In these — the first, middle and last [positions] — where no rule has been laid down, strengthening (`vuddhi`), elision ([`lopa`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)), insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)), modification ([`vikāra`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)), metathesis ([`viparīta`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) and replacement ([`ādesa`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) take place in some places (`kvaci`), without conflicting with the word of the Conqueror. Hence the initial vowel is strengthened even though it is followed by a conjunct, and when the letter `i` is elided `ny` is replaced by `ñ`: `koṇḍañño` ("Kuṇḍanī's son"). Kuru's son is `korabyo`, and here too, by the same rule, the letter `u` is replaced by `ava`; a brother's (`bhātu`) son is `bhātabyo`.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | kuṇḍanī + yā (⑥⨀) + ṇya         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kuṇḍanī + ~~yā~~ + ṇya          | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | kuṇḍanī + (~~ṇ~~ya)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | k + (u→o) + ṇḍanī + ya          | 370 (§404) |
| →   | koṇḍan + (~~ī~~ + ya)           | 369 (§261) |
| →   | koṇḍa + (ny→ñ) + a              | 370 (§404) |
| →   | koṇḍa + (ñ→ññ) + a              | 40 (§28)  |
| →   | koṇḍañña + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | koṇḍañña + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **koṇḍañño**                    |      |

|     | kuru + no (⑥⨀) + ṇya            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kuru + ~~no~~ + ṇya             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | kuru + (~~ṇ~~ya)                | 363 (§396) |
| →   | k + (u→o) + ru + ya             | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | kor + (u→ava) + ya              | 370 (§404) |
| →   | kora + (v→b) + ya               | 370 (§404) |
| →   | korabya + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | korabya + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **korabyo**                     |      |

|     | bhātu + no (⑥⨀) + ṇya           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bhātu + ~~no~~ + ṇya            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | bhātu + (~~ṇ~~ya)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | bhāt + (u→ava) + ya             | 370 (§404) |
| →   | bhāta + (v→b) + ya              | 370 (§404) |
| →   | bhātabya + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | bhātabya + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **bhātabyo**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Upagussa apacca’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis "Upagu's offspring" —

---

<a name="r371"></a>

### 371 (§348): Ṇavopagvādīhi (`ṇava` after `upaku` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§348 (371): Ṇavo’ pakvādīhi" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m348" description="`ṇava` after `upaku` and the like" />
:::

> Upagu manu iccevamādīhi u kārantehi gottagaṇehi ṇava ppaccayo hoti vā ‘‘tassāpacca’’miccetasmiṃ atthe. Ādi saddassa cettha pakāravācakattā u kārantatoyevāyaṃ. Opagavo, opagavī, opagavaṃ, manuno apaccaṃ mānavo, ‘‘mānuso’’ti ṇa ppaccaye, sā game ca kate rūpaṃ, bhagguno apaccaṃ bhaggavo, paṇḍuno apaccaṃ paṇḍavo, upavindussa apaccaṃ opavindavo iccādi.

After clan names (`gottagaṇa`) ending in the letter `u`, of the kind of `upagu` and `manu`, the suffix `ṇava` is optionally used in the sense "his offspring"; and since the word `ādi` here expresses "kind", this suffix comes only after [lemmas] ending in the letter `u`. `opagavo` ("Upagu's offspring"), `opagavī`, `opagavaṃ`; Manu's offspring is `mānavo`, while `mānuso` is the form when the suffix `ṇa` is used and the insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) of `s` is made; Bhaggu's offspring is `bhaggavo`, Paṇḍu's offspring `paṇḍavo`, Upavindu's offspring `opavindavo`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | upagu + ssa (⑥⨀) + ṇava         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | upagu + ~~ssa~~ + ṇava          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | upagu + (~~ṇ~~ava)              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | (u→o) + pagu + ava              | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | opag + (~~u~~ + ava)            | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | opagava + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | opagava + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **opagavo**                     |      |

|     | manu + no (⑥⨀) + ṇa             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | manu + ~~no~~ + ṇa              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | manu + (~~ṇ~~a)                 | 363 (§396) |
| →   | m + (a→ā) + nu + a              | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | mānu + (s) + a                  | 96 (§184) |
| →   | mānusa + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | mānusa + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **mānuso**                      |      |

:::

> ‘‘Vidhavāya apacca’’nti atthe –

In the sense "a widow's offspring" —

---

<a name="r372"></a>

### 372 (§349): Ṇera vidhavādito (`ṇera` after `vidhavā` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§349 (372): Ṇera vidhavādito" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m349" description="`ṇera` after `vidhavā` and the like" />
:::

> Vidhavā dito ṇera ppaccayo hoti vā apaccatthe. Vigato dhavo pati etissāti vidhavā, vedhavero, bandhukiyā abhisāriṇiyā putto bandhukero, samaṇassa upajjhāyassa putto puttaṭṭhāniyattāti sāmaṇero, nāḷikero iccādi.

After `vidhavā` and the like, the suffix `ṇera` is optionally used in the sense of offspring. A `vidhavā` (widow) is a woman whose husband (`dhava`), her lord, is gone; [her son is] `vedhavero` ("a widow's son"). The son of a `bandhukī`, a woman who goes out to her lovers (`abhisāriṇī`), is `bandhukero`; the son of an ascetic (`samaṇa`), that is, of a preceptor, is `sāmaṇero` ("a novice"), because he stands in the place of a son; `nāḷikero`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | vidhavā + āya (⑥⨀) + ṇera       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vidhavā + ~~āya~~ + ṇera        | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | vidhavā + (~~ṇ~~era)            | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (i→e) + dhavā + era         | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | vedhav + (~~ā~~ + era)          | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | vedhavera + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vedhavera + (si→o)              | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **vedhavero**                   |      |

|     | samaṇa + ssa (⑥⨀) + ṇera        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | samaṇa + ~~ssa~~ + ṇera         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | samaṇa + (~~ṇ~~era)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | s + (a→ā) + maṇa + era          | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | sāmaṇ + (~~a~~ + era)           | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | sāmaṇera + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | sāmaṇera + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **sāmaṇero**                    |      |

:::

---

> _Apaccataddhitaṃ._

[Here end] the suffixes of descent.

---

## Saṃsaṭṭhādianekatthataddhita (Suffixes of relation and other senses)

> ‘‘Tilena saṃsaṭṭha’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis "mixed with sesame" —

---

<a name="r373"></a>

### 373 (§350): Yena vā saṃsaṭṭhaṃ tarati carati vahati ṇiko (`ṇika` for "mixed with", "crosses by", "goes by", "carries by" that) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§350 (373): Yena vā saṃsaṭṭhaṃ tarati carati vahati ṇiko" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m350" description="`ṇika` for 'mixed with', 'crosses by', 'goes by', 'carries by' that" />
:::

> Yena vā saṃsaṭṭhaṃ, yena vā tarati, yena vā carati, yena vā vahati, tato tatiyantato liṅgamhā tesu saṃsaṭṭhādīsvatthesu ṇika ppaccayo hoti vā. Telikaṃ bhojanaṃ, tilena abhisaṅkhatanti attho. Telikī yāgu. Guḷena saṃsaṭṭhaṃ goḷikaṃ[^3]. Evaṃ ghātikaṃ, dādhikaṃ, māricikaṃ, loṇikaṃ.

After a lemma ending in the third form ③ — that with which something is mixed, by which one crosses, by which one goes or by which one carries — the suffix `ṇika` is optionally used in those senses, "mixed with" and the rest: `telikaṃ bhojanaṃ` ("food mixed with sesame"), meaning prepared (`abhisaṅkhata`) with sesame; `telikī yāgu` ("gruel mixed with sesame"); mixed with molasses (`guḷa`), `goḷikaṃ`; likewise `ghātikaṃ` ("mixed with ghee"), `dādhikaṃ` ("with curds"), `māricikaṃ` ("with pepper"), `loṇikaṃ` ("with salt").

> Nāvāya taratīti nāviko, uḷumpena taratīti oḷumpiko, vuddhiabhāvapakkhe uḷumpiko. Evaṃ kulliko, gopucchiko. Sakaṭena caratīti sākaṭiko. Evaṃ pādiko, daṇḍiko, dhammena carati pavattatīti dhammiko. Sīsena vahatīti sīsiko, vā ggahaṇena ī kārassa vuddhi na hoti. Evaṃ aṃsiko, khandhiko, hatthiko, aṅguliko.

One who crosses by boat (`nāvā`) is `nāviko`; one who crosses by raft (`uḷumpa`) is `oḷumpiko`, or `uḷumpiko` on the alternative without strengthening; likewise `kulliko` ("one who crosses by a `kulla` raft"), `gopucchiko` ("by a cow's tail"). One who goes by cart (`sakaṭa`) is `sākaṭiko`; likewise `pādiko` ("one who goes on foot"), `daṇḍiko` ("with a staff"); one who goes, that is, proceeds (`pavattati`), by the Dhamma is `dhammiko`. One who carries on the head (`sīsa`) is `sīsiko`, and by the mention of `vā` the letter `ī` is not strengthened; likewise `aṃsiko` ("one who carries on the shoulder"), `khandhiko` ("on the back"), `hatthiko` ("in the hand"), `aṅguliko` ("with the fingers").

> Puna vā ggahaṇena aññatthesupi ṇika ppaccayo, paradāraṃ gacchatīti pāradāriko, pathaṃ gacchatīti pathiko.

By the repeated mention of `vā`, the suffix `ṇika` is used in other senses as well: one who goes to another's wife (`paradāra`) is `pāradāriko`, and one who goes along the road (`patha`) is `pathiko`.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | tila + nā (③⨀) + ṇika           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tila + ~~nā~~ + ṇika            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | tila + (~~ṇ~~ika)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | t + (i→e) + la + ika            | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | tel + (~~a~~ + ika)             | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | telika + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | telika + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **telikaṃ**                     |      |

|     | telika (🚺) + ī                 | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | telik + (~~a~~ + ī)             | 190 (§239), 67 (§83) |
| →   | telikī + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | telikī + ~~si~~                 | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **telikī**                      |      |

|     | uḷumpa + nā (③⨀) + ṇika         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | uḷumpa + ~~nā~~ + ṇika          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | uḷumpa + (~~ṇ~~ika)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | (u→o) + ḷumpa + ika             | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | oḷump + (~~a~~ + ika)           | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | oḷumpika + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | oḷumpika + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **oḷumpiko**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Vinayamadhīte, aveccādhīte’’ti vā viggahe –

In the analysis "he studies the Vinaya", or "he studies [it] thoroughly (`avecca`)" —

> ‘‘Ṇiko’’ti vattate.

“`ṇiko`” continues.

[^3]: CST4 prints `egāḷikaṃ`; `goḷikaṃ` is read with Kaccāyana's vutti under §350.

---

<a name="r374"></a>

### 374 (§351): Tamadhīte tenakatādisannidhānaniyogasippabhaṇḍajīvikatthesu ca (And `ṇika` for "studies that", "made by that" and the like, location, appointment, craft, goods, livelihood) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§351 (374): Tamadhīte tenakatādi sannidhāna niyoga sippa bhaṇḍa jīvikatthesu ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m351" description="And `ṇika` for 'studies that', 'made by that' and the like, location, appointment, craft, goods, livelihood" />
:::

> Catuppadamidaṃ. Tamadhīteti atthe, tena katādīsvatthesu ca tamhi sannidhāno, tattha niyutto, tamassa sippaṃ, tamassa bhaṇḍaṃ, tamassa jīvikā iccetesvatthesu ca dutiyādivibhatyantehi liṅgehi ṇika ppaccayo hoti vā. Venayiko. Evaṃ suttantiko, ābhidhammiko.

This sutta has four words. After lemmas ending in the second ② and the other inflection forms, the suffix `ṇika` is optionally used in the sense "he studies that", in the senses "made by that" and the like, and in the senses "located in that", "appointed to that", "that is his craft", "that is his merchandise" and "that is his livelihood": `venayiko` ("one who studies the Vinaya"); likewise `suttantiko` ("one who studies the Suttanta"), `ābhidhammiko` ("one who studies the Abhidhamma").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | vinaya + aṃ (②⨀) + ṇika         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vinaya + ~~aṃ~~ + ṇika          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | vinaya + (~~ṇ~~ika)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (i→e) + naya + ika          | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | venay + (~~a~~ + ika)           | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | venayika + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | venayika + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **venayiko**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Byākaraṇamadhīte’’ti atthe ṇika ppaccayādimhi kate –

When the suffix `ṇika` and the rest have been applied in the sense "he studies grammar" (`byākaraṇa`) —

> ‘‘Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe cā’’ti vattamāne –

while [364 (§400): Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r364) continues —

---

<a name="r375"></a>

### 375 (§401): Māyūnamāgamo ṭhāne (no `vuddhi` of initial `i`, `u`; the insertion of `e`, `o` in its place) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§401 (375): Māyūnamāgamo ṭhāne" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m401" description="no `vuddhi` of initial `i`, `u`; the insertion of `e`, `o` in its place" />
:::

> I u iccetesaṃ[^4] ādisarānaṃ asaṃyogantānaṃ mā vuddhi hoti saṇe, tatreva vuddhi āgamo hoti ca ṭhāneti ekāravuddhāgamo.

The initial vowels `i` and `u`, when not followed by a conjunct, are not strengthened (`mā vuddhi`) before a `ṇ` [suffix]; instead, right there in its place (`ṭhāne`), the strengthened vowel comes as an insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)). Hence the insertion of the strengthened vowel `e`.

> ‘‘Ṭhāne’’ti vacanā cettha, yū namādesabhūtato; \
> Yave hi pubbeva eo-vuddhiyo honti āgamā.

And here, because of the word `ṭhāne`, it is before what stands as the replacement of `i` and `u`, \
that is, only before `y` and `v`, that the strengthened vowels `e` and `o` come as insertions.

> Ya kārassa dvibhāvo.

The letter `y` is doubled.

> Veyyākaraṇiko, nyāyamadhīteti neyyāyiko. Evaṃ takkiko, vediko, nemittiko, kāyena kato payogo kāyiko, kāyena kataṃ kammaṃ kāyikaṃ, vacasā kataṃ kammaṃ vācasikaṃ. Evaṃ mānasikaṃ, ettha ca ‘‘sasare vāgamo’’ti sutte vavatthitavā saddena paccaye parepi sā gamo, therehi katā saṅgīti therikā. Evaṃ pañcasatikā, sattasatikā, ettha ‘‘ṇavaṇikā’’disutte anuvattitavā ggahaṇena īpaccayo na hoti.

`veyyākaraṇiko` ("one who studies grammar"); one who studies logic (`nyāya`) is `neyyāyiko`; likewise `takkiko` ("one who studies reasoning"), `vediko` ("the Veda"), `nemittiko` ("omens"). An act (`payoga`) done by the body is `kāyiko`, a deed (`kamma`) done by the body `kāyikaṃ`, and a deed done by speech (`vacasā`) `vācasikaṃ`; likewise `mānasikaṃ` ("done by the mind"), and here the insertion of `s` comes even when a suffix follows, by the restricted (`vavatthita`) word `vā` in the sutta [§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/3-tatiyakanda#m184). The recitation (`saṅgīti`) made by the elders (`thera`) is `therikā`; likewise `pañcasatikā` ("made by the five hundred"), `sattasatikā` ("made by the seven hundred"), and here the suffix `ī` is not used, by the mention of `vā` that continues into the sutta [§239 (190): Ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntuhi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/4-catutthakanda#m239).

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | vyākaraṇa (byākaraṇa) + aṃ (②⨀) + ṇika | rule |
| --- | --------------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vyākaraṇa + ~~aṃ~~ + ṇika               | 332 (§317) |
| →   | vyākaraṇa + (~~ṇ~~ika)                  | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (e) + yākaraṇa + ika                | 375 (§401) |
| →   | ve + (y) + yākaraṇa + ika               | 40 (§28)  |
| →   | veyyākaraṇ + (~~a~~ + ika)              | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | veyyākaraṇika + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | veyyākaraṇika + (si→o)                  | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **veyyākaraṇiko**                       |      |

|     | vaca + sā (③⨀) + ṇika           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vaca + ~~sā~~ + ṇika            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | vaca + (~~ṇ~~ika)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (a→ā) + ca + ika            | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | vāca + (s) + ika                | 96 (§184) |
| →   | vācasika + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vācasika + (si→aṃ)              | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **vācasikaṃ**                   |      |

|     | thera + hi (③⨂) + ṇika          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | thera + ~~hi~~ + ṇika           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | thera + (~~ṇ~~ika)              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | ther + (~~a~~ + ika)            | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | therik + (~~a~~ + ā)            | 176 (§237), 67 (§83) |
| →   | therikā + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | therikā + ~~si~~                | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **therikā**                     |      |

:::

> Sannidhānatthe sarīre sannidhānā vedanā sārīrikā, sārīrikaṃ dukkhaṃ. Evaṃ mānasikā, mānasikaṃ.

In the sense of location (`sannidhāna`): a feeling located in the body (`sarīra`) is `sārīrikā`, and `sārīrikaṃ dukkhaṃ` ("bodily pain"); likewise `mānasikā` ("located in the mind"), `mānasikaṃ`.

> Niyuttatthe dvāre niyutto dovāriko, ettha ‘‘māyūnamāgamo ṭhāne’’ti va kārato pubbeo kārāgamo. Evaṃ bhaṇḍāgāriko, nāgariko, navakammiko, vanakammiko, ādikammiko, odariko, rathiko, pathiko, upāye niyutto opāyiko, cetasi niyuttā cetasikā.

In the sense of appointment (`niyutta`): one appointed to the door (`dvāra`) is `dovāriko`, and here the insertion of the letter `o` comes before the letter `v` by “`Māyūnamāgamo ṭhāne`”; likewise `bhaṇḍāgāriko` ("appointed to the storehouse"), `nāgariko` ("to the city"), `navakammiko` ("to building work"), `vanakammiko` ("to forest work"), `ādikammiko` ("engaged in the first deed"), `odariko` ("devoted to the belly"), `rathiko` ("appointed to a chariot"), `pathiko` ("to the road"); one engaged in a means (`upāya`) is `opāyiko`, and [states] engaged in the mind (`cetas`) are `cetasikā`.

> Sippatthe vīṇāvādanaṃ vīṇā, vīṇā assa sippaṃ veṇiko. E vaṃ pāṇaviko, modiṅgiko, vaṃsiko.

In the sense of a craft (`sippa`): `vīṇā` means lute-playing (`vīṇāvādana`), and one whose craft is the lute is `veṇiko`; likewise `pāṇaviko` ("whose craft is the small drum"), `modiṅgiko` ("the `mudiṅga` drum"), `vaṃsiko` ("the bamboo pole").

> Bhaṇḍatthe gandho assa bhaṇḍanti gandhiko. Evaṃ teliko, goḷiko, pūviko, paṇṇiko, tambūliko, loṇiko.

In the sense of merchandise (`bhaṇḍa`): one whose merchandise is perfume (`gandha`) is `gandhiko`; likewise `teliko` ("whose merchandise is oil"), `goḷiko` ("molasses"), `pūviko` ("cakes"), `paṇṇiko` ("greens"), `tambūliko` ("betel"), `loṇiko` ("salt").

> Jīvikatthe urabbhaṃ hantvā jīvati, urabbhamassa jīvikāti vā orabbhiko. Evaṃ māgaviko, ettha va kārāgamo. Sūkariko, sākuṇiko, macchiko iccādi.

In the sense of a livelihood (`jīvikā`): one who lives by killing sheep (`urabbha`), or whose livelihood is sheep, is `orabbhiko`; likewise `māgaviko` ("one who lives by killing deer"), where the letter `v` is inserted; `sūkariko` ("pigs"), `sākuṇiko` ("birds"), `macchiko` ("fish"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | dvāra + smiṃ (⑦⨀) + ṇika        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dvāra + ~~smiṃ~~ + ṇika         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | dvāra + (~~ṇ~~ika)              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | d + (o) + vāra + ika            | 375 (§401) |
| →   | dovār + (~~a~~ + ika)           | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | dovārika + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | dovārika + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **dovāriko**                    |      |

|     | maga + aṃ (②⨀) + ṇika           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | maga + ~~aṃ~~ + ṇika            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | maga + (~~ṇ~~ika)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | m + (a→ā) + ga + ika            | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | māga + (v) + ika                | 370 (§404) |
| →   | māgavika + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | māgavika + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **māgaviko**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Tena katādī’’ti ettha ādi ggahaṇena tena hataṃ, tena baddhaṃ, tena kītaṃ, tena dibbati, so assa āvudho, so assa ābādho, tattha pasanno, tassa santakaṃ, tamassa parimāṇaṃ, tassa rāsi, taṃ arahati, tamassa sīlaṃ, tattha jāto, tattha vasati, tatra vidito, tadatthāya saṃvattati, tato āgato, tato sambhūto, tadassa payojananti evamādiatthe ca ṇika ppaccayo hoti. Yathā – jālena hato, hanatīti vā jāliko. Evaṃ bāḷisiko, vākariko, suttena baddho suttiko, varattāya baddho vārattiko nāgo.

By the mention of `ādi` in “`tena katādi`” ("made by that, and the like"), the suffix `ṇika` is used also in such senses as these: killed by that, bound with that, bought with that, plays with that, that is his weapon, that is his ailment, devoted to that, the property of that, that is its measure, a heap of that, deserves that, that is his habit, born there, dwells there, known there, conduces to that, come from there, arisen from there, that is its purpose. For instance, one killed with a net (`jāla`), or who kills with one, is `jāliko`; likewise `bāḷisiko` ("one who fishes with a hook"), `vākariko` ("one who hunts with a snare"); one bound with a thread (`sutta`) is `suttiko`, and an elephant bound with a leather strap (`varattā`) is `vārattiko nāgo`.

> Vatthena kītaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ vatthikaṃ. Evaṃ kumbhikaṃ, phālikaṃ, sovaṇṇikaṃ, sātikaṃ. Akkhena dibbatīti akkhiko. Evaṃ sālākiko, tindukiko, ambaphaliko. Cāpo assa āvudhoti cāpiko. Evaṃ tomariko, muggariko, mosaliko.

Goods bought with cloth (`vattha`) are `vatthikaṃ`; likewise `kumbhikaṃ` ("bought with a pot"), `phālikaṃ` ("with a ploughshare"), `sovaṇṇikaṃ` ("with gold"), `sātikaṃ` ("with a hundred"). One who plays with dice (`akkha`) is `akkhiko`; likewise `sālākiko` ("one who plays with sticks"), `tindukiko` ("with `tinduka` fruits"), `ambaphaliko` ("with mangoes"). One whose weapon is the bow (`cāpa`) is `cāpiko`; likewise `tomariko` ("whose weapon is the goad"), `muggariko` ("the club"), `mosaliko` ("the pestle").

> Vāto assa ābādhoti vātiko. Evaṃ semhiko, pittiko.

One whose ailment is wind (`vāta`) is `vātiko`; likewise `semhiko` ("whose ailment is phlegm"), `pittiko` ("bile").

> Buddhe pasanno buddhiko. Evaṃ dhammiko, saṅghiko. Buddhassa santako buddhiko. Evaṃ dhammiko, saṅghiko vihāro, saṅghikā bhūmi, saṅghikaṃ cīvaraṃ, puggalikaṃ.

One devoted to the Buddha is `buddhiko`; likewise `dhammiko` ("devoted to the Dhamma"), `saṅghiko` ("to the Saṅgha"). What is the Buddha's property is `buddhiko`; likewise `dhammiko` ("the Dhamma's"), `saṅghiko vihāro` ("a monastery belonging to the Saṅgha"), `saṅghikā bhūmi` ("land belonging to the Saṅgha"), `saṅghikaṃ cīvaraṃ` ("a robe belonging to the Saṅgha"), `puggalikaṃ` ("belonging to an individual").

> Kumbho assa parimāṇanti kumbhikaṃ. Evaṃ khārikaṃ, doṇikaṃ. Kumbhassa rāsi kumbhiko. Kumbhaṃ arahatīti kumbhiko. Evaṃ doṇiko, aṭṭhamāsiko, kahāpaṇiko, āsītikā gāthā, nāvutikā, sātikaṃ, sāhassikaṃ. Sandiṭṭhamarahatīti sandiṭṭhiko, ‘‘ehi passā’’ti imaṃ vidhiṃ arahatīti ehipassiko.

That whose measure is a pot (`kumbha`) is `kumbhikaṃ`; likewise `khārikaṃ` ("measuring a `khārī`"), `doṇikaṃ` ("measuring a `doṇa`"). A heap of a pot is `kumbhiko`. What deserves a pot is `kumbhiko`; likewise `doṇiko` ("worth a `doṇa`"), `aṭṭhamāsiko` ("worth eight `māsaka`s"), `kahāpaṇiko` ("worth a `kahāpaṇa`"), `āsītikā gāthā` ("a verse worth eighty"), `nāvutikā` ("worth ninety"), `sātikaṃ` ("worth a hundred"), `sāhassikaṃ` ("worth a thousand"). What deserves to be seen for oneself (`sandiṭṭha`) is `sandiṭṭhiko`, and what deserves this procedure ([`vidhi`](/introduction#types-of-suttas)), "come, see" (`ehi passa`), is `ehipassiko`.

> Sīlatthe paṃsukūladhāraṇaṃ paṃsukūlaṃ, taṃ sīlamassāti paṃsukūliko. Evaṃ tecīvariko, ekāsane bhojanasīlo ekāsaniko, rukkhamūle vasanasīlo rukkhamūliko, tathā āraññiko, sosāniko.

In the sense of a habit (`sīla`): `paṃsukūla` means the wearing of rag-robes, and one who has that as his habit is `paṃsukūliko`; likewise `tecīvariko` ("whose habit is the three robes"); one whose habit is eating at a single sitting is `ekāsaniko`, and one whose habit is dwelling at the root of a tree `rukkhamūliko`; so too `āraññiko` ("whose habit is dwelling in the forest"), `sosāniko` ("in a charnel ground").

> Jātatthe apāye jāto āpāyiko. Evaṃ nerayiko, sāmuddiko maccho, vassesu jāto vassiko, vassikā, vassikaṃ pupphaṃ, sāradiko, hemantiko, vāsantiko, cātuddasiko, rājagahe jāto, rājagahe vasatīti vā rājagahiko jano, magadhesu jāto, vasatīti vā māgadhiko, māgadhikā, māgadhikaṃ, sāvatthiyaṃ jāto, vasatīti vā sāvatthiko, kāpilavatthiko, vesāliko.

In the sense of birth (`jāta`): one born in a state of loss (`apāya`) is `āpāyiko`; likewise `nerayiko` ("born in hell"), `sāmuddiko maccho` ("a fish born in the sea"); born in the rains (`vassa`), `vassiko`, `vassikā`, `vassikaṃ pupphaṃ` ("a flower born in the rains"); `sāradiko` ("born in autumn"), `hemantiko` ("in winter"), `vāsantiko` ("in spring"), `cātuddasiko` ("on the fourteenth day"); a person born in Rājagaha, or who dwells in Rājagaha, is `rājagahiko jano`; born, or dwelling, among the Magadhans, `māgadhiko`, `māgadhikā`, `māgadhikaṃ`; born, or dwelling, in Sāvatthī, `sāvatthiko`; `kāpilavatthiko` ("of Kapilavatthu"), `vesāliko` ("of Vesālī").

> Loke vidito lokiko, lokāya saṃvattatītipi lokiko. Tathā mātito āgataṃ mātikaṃ, pitito āgataṃ pettikaṃ nāmaṃ.

What is known in the world (`loka`) is `lokiko`, and what conduces to the world is also `lokiko`. So too, what has come from the mother (`mātito`) is `mātikaṃ`, and a name that has come from the father is `pettikaṃ nāmaṃ`.

> Sambhūtatthe mātito sambhūtaṃ mattikaṃ. Evaṃ pettikaṃ. Upadhitassa payojanaṃ opadhikaṃ.

In the sense of origin (`sambhūta`): what has arisen from the mother is `mattikaṃ`; likewise `pettikaṃ` ("arisen from the father"). That whose purpose is acquisition (`upadhi`) is `opadhikaṃ`.

> Sakatthepi asaṅkhāroyeva asaṅkhārikaṃ. Evaṃ sasaṅkhārikaṃ, nāmameva nāmikaṃ. Evaṃ ākhyātikaṃ, opasaggikaṃ, nepātikaṃ, catumahārāje bhatti etesanti cātumahārājikā. Evaṃ aññatthepi yojetabbaṃ.

Also in the word's own sense (`sakattha`): `asaṅkhārikaṃ` is just `asaṅkhāro` ("unprompted"); likewise `sasaṅkhārikaṃ` ("prompted"); `nāmikaṃ` is just `nāma` ("a noun"); likewise `ākhyātikaṃ` ("a verb"), `opasaggikaṃ` ("a prefix"), `nepātikaṃ` ("a particle"); those whose devotion (`bhatti`) is to the Four Great Kings are `cātumahārājikā`. It should be applied in the same way in other senses too.

:::tip[Examples]

> `svākkhāto bhagavatā dhammo sandiṭṭhiko akāliko ehipassiko opaneyyiko` \
> [(7D 3.10: Dhammādāsadhammapariyāya)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/3/3.10#335) \
🚹①⨀(svākkhāta) 🚹③⨀(bhagavantu) 🚹①⨀(dhamma) 🚹①⨀(sandiṭṭha + ṇika) 🚹①⨀(akālika) 🚹①⨀(ehipassa + ṇika) 🚹①⨀(upaneyya + ṇika) \
well proclaimed | by the Blessed One | the Dhamma | visible here and now | timeless | inviting one to come and see | leading onward

:::

> ‘‘Kasāvena ratta’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis "dyed with the brown dye" (`kasāva`) —

[^4]: CST4 prints `iccebhesaṃ`; Buddhappiya's word is `iccetesaṃ`, as in the next sutta.

---

<a name="r376"></a>

### 376 (§352): Ṇa rāgā tenarattaṃ tassedamaññatthesu ca (`ṇa` after a dye for "dyed with that"; and for "this is his" and other senses) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§352 (376): Ṇa rāgā tasse damaññatthesu ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m352" description="`ṇa` after a dye for 'dyed with that'; and for 'this is his' and other senses" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Buddhappiya's sutta has `tena rattaṃ` ("dyed with that"), which the CST text of Kaccāyana's §352 lacks and its vutti supplies.

:::

> Rāgatthavācakā liṅgamhā ‘‘tena ratta’’miccetasmiṃ atthe, ‘‘tasse’’ti chaṭṭhiyantato ‘‘ida’’miccetasmiṃ atthe ca aññatthesu ca ṇa ppaccayo hoti vā.

The suffix `ṇa` is optionally used after a lemma that expresses a dye (`rāga`) in the sense "dyed with that", after a lemma ending in the sixth form ⑥ — `tassa` ("his") — in the sense "this [is his]", and in other senses too.

> Kāsāvaṃ vatthaṃ. Evaṃ kāsāyaṃ, kusumbhena rattaṃ kosumbhaṃ, haliddiyā rattaṃ hāliddaṃ, pattaṅgaṃ, mañjiṭṭhaṃ, kuṅkumaṃ, nīlena rattaṃ nīlaṃ. Evaṃ pītaṃ.

`kāsāvaṃ vatthaṃ` ("cloth dyed with the brown dye"); likewise `kāsāyaṃ`; dyed with safflower (`kusumbha`), `kosumbhaṃ`; dyed with turmeric (`haliddī`), `hāliddaṃ`; `pattaṅgaṃ` ("dyed with red sandal"), `mañjiṭṭhaṃ` ("with madder"), `kuṅkumaṃ` ("with saffron"); dyed with blue (`nīla`), `nīlaṃ`; likewise `pītaṃ` ("dyed yellow").

> Idamatthe mahiṃsassa idaṃ māhiṃsaṃ maṃsaṃ, dadhi sappi cammādikaṃ vā, sūkarassa idaṃ sūkaraṃ, kaccāyanassa idaṃ kaccāyanaṃ byākaraṇaṃ. Evaṃ sogataṃ sāsanaṃ.

In the sense "this [is his]": what is the buffalo's (`mahiṃsa`) is `māhiṃsaṃ maṃsaṃ` ("buffalo meat"), or its curds, ghee, hide and the like; what is the pig's (`sūkara`) is `sūkaraṃ`; what is Kaccāyana's is `kaccāyanaṃ byākaraṇaṃ` ("the Kaccāyana grammar"); likewise `sogataṃ sāsanaṃ` ("the Sugata's dispensation").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | kasāva + nā (③⨀) + ṇa           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kasāva + ~~nā~~ + ṇa            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | kasāva + (~~ṇ~~a)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | k + (a→ā) + sāva + a            | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | kāsāv + (~~a~~ + a)             | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | kāsāva + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kāsāva + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **kāsāvaṃ**                     |      |

|     | sugata + ssa (⑥⨀) + ṇa          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sugata + ~~ssa~~ + ṇa           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | sugata + (~~ṇ~~a)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | s + (u→o) + gata + a            | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | sogat + (~~a~~ + a)             | 67 (§83)  |
| →   | sogata + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | sogata + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **sogataṃ**                     |      |

:::

> ‘‘Isissa ida’’nti atthe ṇa ppaccaye kate vuddhimhi sampatte –

When the suffix `ṇa` has been added in the sense "this is the sage's" (`isissa idaṃ`) and the strengthening would apply —

> ‘‘Saṇe, yūnamāgamo ṭhāne’’ti ca vattate.

And “`saṇe`” and “`yūnamāgamo ṭhāne`” continue.

---

<a name="r377"></a>

### 377 (§402): Āttañca (and `ā` [for initial `i`, `u`], with the insertion of `ri`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§402 (377): Āttañca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m402" description="and `ā` [for initial `i`, `u`], with the insertion of `ri`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

“`saṇe`” continues from [364 (§400): Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r364), and “`yūnamāgamo ṭhāne`” from [375 (§401): Māyūnamāgamo ṭhāne](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r375).

:::

> I u iccetesaṃ ādisarānaṃ ā ttañca hoti saṇa kārappaccaye pare, ca saddena ri kārāgamo ca ṭhāneti i kārassa ā ttaṃ.

The initial vowels `i` and `u` also become `ā` when a suffix with the letter `ṇ` follows, and by the word `ca` there is also the insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) of the letter `ri`, in its place (`ṭhāne`). Hence the letter `i` becomes `ā`.

> Ṭhānādhikārato āttaṃ, isūsabhaujādinaṃ; \
> Isissa tu rikārāga-mo cāttānantare bhave.

By the governing rule ([`adhikāra`](/introduction#types-of-suttas)) `ṭhāne`, `isi`, `usabha`, `uju` and the like take `ā`; \
but for `isi` the letter `ri` is inserted as well, immediately after the `ā`.

> Ārisyaṃ, usabhassa idaṃ āsabhaṃ ṭhānaṃ, āsabhī vācā.

`ārisyaṃ` ("the sage's"); this belongs to the bull (`usabhassa idaṃ`): `āsabhaṃ ṭhānaṃ` ("the bull's place, the position of a leader"), `āsabhī vācā` ("bull-like, lordly speech").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | isi + ssa (⑥⨀) + ṇa             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | isi + ~~ssa~~ + ṇa              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | isi + (~~ṇ~~a)                  | 363 (§396) |
| →   | (i→ā) + si + a                  | 377 (§402) |
| →   | ā + (ri) + si + a               | 377 (§402) |
| →   | āris + (i→y) + a                | 21 (§21) |
| →   | ārisya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | ārisya + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **ārisyaṃ**                     |      |

|     | usabha + ssa (⑥⨀) + ṇa          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | usabha + ~~ssa~~ + ṇa           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | usabha + (~~ṇ~~a)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | (u→ā) + sabha + a               | 377 (§402) |
| →   | āsabh + (~~a~~ + a)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | āsabha + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | āsabha + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **āsabhaṃ**                     |      |

:::

> Aññattha ggahaṇena pana avidūrabhavo, tatra bhavo, tatra jāto, tato āgato, so assa nivāso, tassa issaro, kattikādīhi niyutto māso, sāssa devatā, tamaveccādhīte, tassa visayo deso, tasmiṃ dese atthi, tena nibbattaṃ, taṃ arahati, tassa vikāro, tamassa parimāṇanti iccevamādīsvatthesu ca ṇa ppaccayo. Yathā – vidisāya avidūre bhavo vediso gāmo, udumbarassa avidūre bhavaṃ odumbaraṃ vimānaṃ.

By the mention of `aññattha` ("in other senses"), however, the suffix `ṇa` is used also in senses such as these: existing not far from that, existing there, born there, come from there, that is his dwelling, lord of that, the month joined with Kattikā and the rest, that is his deity, studies that thoroughly, the region that is the domain of that [people], [something] exists in that region, produced by that, deserves that, a modification of that, that is its measure. For example, a village that exists not far from Vidisā is `vediso gāmo`, and a palace that exists not far from a fig tree (`udumbara`) is `odumbaraṃ vimānaṃ`.

> Bhavatthe manasi bhavaṃ mānasaṃ sukhaṃ, sāgamo. Sare bhavo sāraso sakuṇo, sārasā sakuṇī, sārasaṃ pupphaṃ, urasi bhavo oraso putto, urasi saṃvaḍḍhitattā, mitte bhavā mettā, mettī vā, pure bhavā porī vācā.

In the sense "existing in": happiness that exists in the mind (`manasi`) is `mānasaṃ sukhaṃ`, with the insertion of `s`. A bird that exists on a lake (`sare`) is `sāraso sakuṇo` ("a water bird"); so too `sārasā sakuṇī` ("a female water bird") and `sārasaṃ pupphaṃ` ("a lake flower"). A son who exists at the breast (`urasi`) is `oraso putto` ("one's own son"), because he is reared at the breast. What exists in a friend (`mitte`) is `mettā` ("friendliness"), or `mettī`; and speech that exists in the city (`pure`) is `porī vācā` ("urbane speech").

> Jātādīsu pāvuse jāto pāvuso megho, pāvusā ratti, pāvusaṃ abbhaṃ, sarade jāto sārado māso, sāradā ratti, sāradaṃ pupphaṃ. Evaṃ sisiro, hemanto, vasanto, gimho[^5], mathurāyaṃ jāto māthuro jano, māthurā gaṇikā, māthuraṃ vatthaṃ. Mathurāya āgato māthuro, mathurā assa nivāsoti māthuro, mathurāya issaro māthuro rājā. ‘‘Sabbato ko’’ti ettha puna sabbato ggahaṇena taddhitatopi kvaci sasaraka kārāgamo, māthurako vā, rājagahe jāto, rājagahā āgato, rājagaho assa nivāsoti vā, rājagahassa issaroti vā rājagaho, rājagahako vā. Evaṃ sāgalo, sāgalako vā, pāṭaliputto, pāṭaliputtako vā, vesāliyaṃ jātotiādiatthe vesālo, vesālako vā, kusināre jāto kosināro, kosinārako vā. Evaṃ sāketo, sāketako vā, kosambo, kosambako vā, indapatto, indapattako vā, kapillo, kapillako vā, bhārukaccho, bhārukacchako vā, nagare jāto, nagarā āgato, nagare vasatīti vā nāgaro, nāgarako vā. Evaṃ jānapado.

In the senses "born in" and the rest: a cloud born in the rainy season (`pāvuse`) is `pāvuso megho`; so too `pāvusā ratti` ("a night of the rains") and `pāvusaṃ abbhaṃ` ("a rain cloud"); a month born in autumn (`sarade`) is `sārado māso`, and so `sāradā ratti` ("an autumn night") and `sāradaṃ pupphaṃ` ("an autumn flower"). Likewise `sisiro` ("of the cool season"), `hemanto` ("of winter"), `vasanto` ("of spring") and `gimho` ("of the hot season"). A person born in Mathurā is `māthuro jano`; so too `māthurā gaṇikā` ("a courtesan of Mathurā") and `māthuraṃ vatthaṃ` ("cloth of Mathurā"). One come from Mathurā is `māthuro`; one whose dwelling is Mathurā is `māthuro`; the lord of Mathurā is `māthuro rājā`. Because `sabbato` is mentioned again in [§178 (224): Sabbato ko](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/3-tatiyakanda#m178), the letter `ka` with its vowel is inserted in some places (`kvaci`) even after a `taddhita` [suffix]: `māthurako` as well. One born in Rājagaha, come from Rājagaha, whose dwelling is Rājagaha, or the lord of Rājagaha, is `rājagaho` or `rājagahako`. Likewise `sāgalo` or `sāgalako` ("of Sāgala"), `pāṭaliputto` or `pāṭaliputtako` ("of Pāṭaliputta"); in the senses "born in Vesālī" and the rest, `vesālo` or `vesālako`; one born in Kusinārā is `kosināro` or `kosinārako`. Likewise `sāketo` or `sāketako` ("of Sāketa"), `kosambo` or `kosambako` ("of Kosambī"), `indapatto` or `indapattako` ("of Indapatta"), `kapillo` or `kapillako` ("of Kapilla"), `bhārukaccho` or `bhārukacchako` ("of Bhārukaccha"); one born in a city (`nagare`), come from a city, or dwelling in a city, is `nāgaro` or `nāgarako`. Likewise `jānapado` ("of the country").

> Janapadanāmesu pana sabbattha bahuvacanameva bhavati. Yathā – aṅgesu jāto, aṅgehi āgato, aṅgā assa nivāso, aṅgānaṃ issaro vā aṅgo, aṅgako vā, māgadho, māgadhako vā, kosalo, kosalako vā, vedeho, vedehako vā, kambojo, kambojako vā, gandhāro, gandhārako vā, sovīro, sovīrako vā, sindhavo, sindhavako vā, assako, kāliṅgo, pañcālo, sakko, tathā suraṭṭhe jāto, suraṭṭhassa issaro vā soraṭṭho, soraṭṭhako vā. Evaṃ mahāraṭṭho, mahāraṭṭhako vā iccādi.

In the names of countries, however, it is in every case only the plural that is used: one born among the Aṅgas (`aṅgesu`), come from the Aṅgas (`aṅgehi`), whose dwelling is the Aṅgas (`aṅgā`), or lord of the Aṅgas (`aṅgānaṃ`), is `aṅgo` or `aṅgako`; likewise `māgadho` or `māgadhako` ("of the Magadhans"), `kosalo` or `kosalako` ("of the Kosalans"), `vedeho` or `vedehako` ("of the Videhans"), `kambojo` or `kambojako` ("of the Kambojans"), `gandhāro` or `gandhārako` ("of the Gandhārans"), `sovīro` or `sovīrako` ("of the Sovīras"), `sindhavo` or `sindhavako` ("of the Sindhus"), `assako` ("of the Assakas"), `kāliṅgo` ("of the Kaliṅgas"), `pañcālo` ("of the Pañcālas"), `sakko` ("of the Sakyans"); so too one born in Suraṭṭha, or the lord of Suraṭṭha, is `soraṭṭho` or `soraṭṭhako`. Likewise `mahāraṭṭho` or `mahāraṭṭhako` ("of Mahāraṭṭha"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | mathurā + yaṃ (⑦⨀) + ṇa         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mathurā + ~~yaṃ~~ + ṇa          | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | mathurā + (~~ṇ~~a)              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | m + (a→ā) + thurā + a           | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | māthur + (~~ā~~ + a)            | 67 (§83) |
| →   | māthura + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | māthura + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | māthura + (ka) + o              | 224 (§178) (optional) |
| →   | **māthuro**, **māthurako**      |      |

|     | mana + smiṃ (⑦⨀) + ṇa           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + ~~smiṃ~~ + ṇa            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | mana + (~~ṇ~~a)                 | 363 (§396) |
| →   | m + (a→ā) + na + a              | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | māna + (s) + a                  | 96 (§184) |
| →   | mānasa + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | mānasa + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **mānasaṃ**                     |      |

:::

> Nakkhattayoge kattikāya puṇṇacandayuttāya yutto māso kattiko, magasirena candayuttena nakkhattena yutto māso māgasiro. Evaṃ phussena yutto māso phusso, maghāya yutto māso māgho, phagguniyā yutto māso phagguno, cittāya yutto māso citto, visākhāya yutto māso vesākho, jeṭṭhāya yutto māso jeṭṭho, uttarāsāḷhāya yutto māso āsāḷho, āsāḷhī vā, savaṇena yutto māso sāvaṇo, sāvaṇī. Bhaddena yutto māso bhaddo, assayujena yutto māso assayujo, buddho assa devatāti buddho. Evaṃ sogato, māhindo, yāmo, somo.

In the sense of conjunction with a constellation (`nakkhattayoga`): the month joined with Kattikā when she is joined with the full moon is `kattiko`; the month joined with the constellation Magasira when it is joined with the moon is `māgasiro`. Likewise the month joined with Phussa is `phusso`, with Maghā `māgho`, with Phaggunī `phagguno`, with Cittā `citto`, with Visākhā `vesākho`, with Jeṭṭhā `jeṭṭho`, with Uttarāsāḷhā `āsāḷho` or `āsāḷhī`, with Savaṇa `sāvaṇo`, `sāvaṇī`, with Bhadda `bhaddo`, and with Assayuja `assayujo`. One whose deity is the Buddha is `buddho`; likewise `sogato` ("whose deity is the Sugata"), `māhindo` ("whose deity is Mahinda"), `yāmo` ("whose deity is Yama") and `somo` ("whose deity is Soma").

> Byākaraṇaṃ aveccādhīte veyyākaraṇo. Evaṃ mohutto, nemitto, aṅgavijjo, vatthuvijjo. Vasātīnaṃ visayo deso vāsāto, udumbarā asmiṃ padese santīti odumbaro deso.

One who studies grammar (`byākaraṇaṃ`) thoroughly (`avecca`) is `veyyākaraṇo` ("a grammarian"); likewise `mohutto` ("one who studies the hours, an astrologer"), `nemitto` ("one who studies omens, a diviner"), `aṅgavijjo` ("one who studies the lore of bodily marks") and `vatthuvijjo` ("one who studies the lore of sites"). The region that is the domain of the Vasātīs is `vāsāto`, and a region in which there are fig trees (`udumbarā`) is `odumbaro deso`.

> Sahassena nibbattā sāhassī parikhā, payasā nibbattaṃ pāyāsaṃ, sahassaṃ arahatīti sāhassī gāthā, ayaso vikāro āyaso. Evaṃ sovaṇṇo, puriso parimāṇamassāti porisaṃ udakaṃ.

A moat produced with a thousand (`sahassena`) is `sāhassī parikhā`; what is produced from milk (`payasā`) is `pāyāsaṃ` ("milk-rice"); a verse that deserves a thousand is `sāhassī gāthā`; a modification of iron (`ayaso`) is `āyaso` ("made of iron"), and likewise `sovaṇṇo` ("made of gold"); water whose measure is a man (`puriso`) is `porisaṃ udakaṃ`.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | visākhā + āya (③⨀) + ṇa         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | visākhā + ~~āya~~ + ṇa          | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | visākhā + (~~ṇ~~a)              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v + (i→e) + sākhā + a           | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | vesākh + (~~ā~~ + a)            | 67 (§83) |
| →   | vesākha + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vesākha + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **vesākho**                     |      |

:::

> Ca ggahaṇena tattha jāto, tattha vasati, tassa hitaṃ, taṃ arahatītiādīsu ṇeyya ppaccayo. Bārāṇasiyaṃ jāto, vasatīti vā bārāṇaseyyako, pure viya ka kārāgamo. Evaṃ campeyyako, sāgaleyyako, mithileyyako jano, gaṅgeyyo maccho, silāya jātaṃ seleyyakaṃ, kule jāto koleyyako sunakho, vane jātaṃ vāneyyaṃ pupphaṃ. Evaṃ pabbateyyo mānuso, pabbateyyā nadī, pabbateyyaṃ osadhaṃ, pathassa hitaṃ pātheyyaṃ, sapatissa hitaṃ sāpateyyaṃ dhanaṃ, padīpeyyaṃ telaṃ, mātu hitaṃ matteyyaṃ. Evaṃ petteyyaṃ. Dakkhiṇamarahatīti dakkhiṇeyyo iccādi.

By the mention of `ca`, the suffix `ṇeyya` is used in the senses "born there", "dwells there", "good for that", "deserves that" and the like. One born, or dwelling, in Bārāṇasī is `bārāṇaseyyako`, with the letter `ka` inserted as before; likewise `campeyyako` ("of Campā"), `sāgaleyyako` ("of Sāgala"), `mithileyyako jano` ("a man of Mithilā") and `gaṅgeyyo maccho` ("a fish of the Ganges"). What is born on rock (`silāya`) is `seleyyakaṃ` ("lichen"); a dog born in a [good] family (`kule`) is `koleyyako sunakho` ("a dog of good breed"); a flower born in the forest (`vane`) is `vāneyyaṃ pupphaṃ`. Likewise `pabbateyyo mānuso` ("a man of the mountains"), `pabbateyyā nadī` ("a mountain river") and `pabbateyyaṃ osadhaṃ` ("a mountain herb"). What is good for the road (`pathassa`) is `pātheyyaṃ` ("provisions for a journey"); wealth that is good for its owner (`sapatissa`) is `sāpateyyaṃ dhanaṃ` ("one's own property"); `padīpeyyaṃ telaṃ` ("oil for the lamp"); what is good for one's mother (`mātu`) is `matteyyaṃ`, and likewise `petteyyaṃ` ("good for one's father"). One who deserves an offering (`dakkhiṇaṃ`) is `dakkhiṇeyyo`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | gaṅgā + yaṃ (⑦⨀) + ṇeyya        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | gaṅgā + ~~yaṃ~~ + ṇeyya         | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | gaṅgā + (~~ṇ~~eyya)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | gaṅg + (~~ā~~ + eyya)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | gaṅgeyya + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | gaṅgeyya + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **gaṅgeyyo**                    |      |

|     | bārāṇasī + yaṃ (⑦⨀) + ṇeyya     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bārāṇasī + ~~yaṃ~~ + ṇeyya      | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | bārāṇasī + (~~ṇ~~eyya)          | 363 (§396) |
| →   | bārāṇas + (~~ī~~ + eyya)        | 67 (§83) |
| →   | bārāṇaseyya + (ka) + (si→o)     | 224 (§178), 66 (§104) |
| →   | **bārāṇaseyyako**               |      |

:::

[^5]: CST4 prints `vimho`; `gimho`, from `gimha`, the hot season, is read.

---

<a name="r378"></a>

### 378 (§353): Jātādīnamimiyā ca (And `ima`, `iya` in the sense "born" and the like) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§353 (378): Jātādīnamimiyā ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m353" description="And `ima`, `iya` in the sense 'born' and the like" />
:::

> Jāta iccevamādīnaṃ saddānaṃ atthe ima iya iccete paccayā honti vā.

In the sense of the words `jāta` ("born") and the like, the suffixes `ima` and `iya` are optionally used.

> Pacchā jāto pacchimo, pacchimā janatā, pacchimaṃ cittaṃ, ante jāto antimo, antimā, antimaṃ. Evaṃ majjhimo, purimo, uparimo, heṭṭhimo, paccantimo, gopphimo, ganthimo.

One born after (`pacchā`) is `pacchimo` ("last"); so too `pacchimā janatā` ("later people") and `pacchimaṃ cittaṃ` ("the last thought"); one born at the end (`ante`) is `antimo` ("final"), `antimā`, `antimaṃ`. Likewise `majjhimo` ("middle"), `purimo` ("former"), `uparimo` ("upper"), `heṭṭhimo` ("lower"), `paccantimo` ("of the border"), `gopphimo` ("at the ankle") and `ganthimo` ("tied").

> Tathā iya ppaccaye manussajātiyā jāto manussajātiyo, manussajātiyā, manussajātiyaṃ. Evaṃ assajātiyo, hatthijātiyo, bodhisattajātiyo, dabba jātiyo, samānajātiyo, lokiyo iccādi.

So too with the suffix `iya`: one born of the human kind (`manussajātiyā`) is `manussajātiyo`, `manussajātiyā`, `manussajātiyaṃ`. Likewise `assajātiyo` ("of the horse kind"), `hatthijātiyo` ("of the elephant kind"), `bodhisattajātiyo` ("of the Bodhisatta's kind"), `dabbajātiyo` ("of the kind of a substance"), `samānajātiyo` ("of the same kind") and `lokiyo` ("worldly"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | pacchā + ima                    | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pacch + (~~ā~~ + ima)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | pacchima + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pacchima + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **pacchimo**                    |      |

|     | manussajāti + yā (⑥⨀) + iya     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | manussajāti + ~~yā~~ + iya      | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | manussajāt + (~~i~~ + iya)      | 67 (§83) |
| →   | manussajātiya + si              | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | manussajātiya + (si→o)          | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **manussajātiyo**               |      |

:::

> Ādi ggahaṇena tattha niyutto, tadassa atthi, tattha bhavotiādīsvapi ima iya ppaccayā honti, ca saddena ika ppaccayo ca. Ante niyutto antimo, antiyo, antiko, putto assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatīti puttimo, puttiyo, puttiko[^6], kappo assa atthīti kappiyo, jaṭā assa atthīti jaṭiyo, hānabhāgo assa atthīti hānabhāgiyo. Evaṃ ṭhitibhāgiyo, bodhissa pakkhe bhavā bodhipakkhiyā, pañcavagge bhavā pañcavaggiyā. Evaṃ chabbaggiyā, udariyaṃ, attano idanti attaniyaṃ, nakārāgamo.

By the mention of `ādi` ("and the like"), the suffixes `ima` and `iya` are used also in the senses "assigned to that", "he has that", "existing in that" and the like, and by the word `ca` the suffix `ika` as well. One assigned to the end (`ante`) is `antimo`, `antiyo`, `antiko`; one who has a son (`putto`), or in whom a son is found, is `puttimo`, `puttiyo`, `puttiko`; what has fitness (`kappo`) is `kappiyo` ("allowable"); one who has matted hair (`jaṭā`) is `jaṭiyo`; what has a share in decline (`hānabhāgo`) is `hānabhāgiyo`, and likewise `ṭhitibhāgiyo` ("sharing in stability"); [states] existing on the side of awakening (`bodhissa pakkhe`) are `bodhipakkhiyā`; those existing in the group of five (`pañcavagge`) are `pañcavaggiyā`, and likewise `chabbaggiyā` ("of the group of six") and `udariyaṃ` ("what is in the stomach"); what is one's own (`attano idaṃ`) is `attaniyaṃ`, with the insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) of the letter `n`.

> Ca saddaggahaṇena kiya yaṇya ppaccayā ca. Jātiyā niyutto jātikiyo. Evaṃ andhakiyo, jaccandhe niyutto jaccandhakiyo. Sassa ayanti sakiyo. Evaṃ parakiyo.

By the mention of the word `ca`, the suffixes `kiya`, `ya` and `ṇya` as well. One assigned to a class (`jātiyā`) is `jātikiyo`; likewise `andhakiyo` ("assigned to the blind"); one assigned to those blind from birth (`jaccandhe`) is `jaccandhakiyo`. What belongs to oneself (`sassa ayaṃ`) is `sakiyo`, and likewise `parakiyo` ("another's").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | atta + no (⑥⨀) + iya            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | atta + ~~no~~ + iya             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | atta + (n) + iya                | 370 (§404) |
| →   | attaniya + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | attaniya + (si→aṃ)              | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **attaniyaṃ**                   |      |

:::

> Ya ppaccayo sādhuhitabhavajātā diatthesu. Yathā – kammani sādhu kammaññaṃ. Sabhāyaṃ sādhu sabbhaṃ, ‘‘yavataṃ talanā’’dinā u kārādi[^7]. Evaṃ medhāya hitaṃ mejjhaṃ ghaṭaṃ. Pādānaṃ hitaṃ pajjaṃ telaṃ, rathassa hitā racchā, gāme bhavo gammo, gave bhavaṃ gabyaṃ, ‘‘osare cā’’ti sutte ca saddena ya ppaccaye parepi avā deso. Kavimhi bhavaṃ kabyaṃ, divi bhavā dibyā, tha nato jātaṃ thaññaṃ, dhanāya saṃvattatīti dhaññaṃ.

The suffix `ya` is used in the senses "good at", "good for", "existing in", "born of" and the like. For example, what is good at work (`kammani`) is `kammaññaṃ` ("fit for work"); what is good in an assembly (`sabhāyaṃ`) is `sabbhaṃ`, by [§269 (41): Yavataṃ ta la ṇa dakārānaṃ byañjanāni ca la ña jakārattaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m269) and the rest. Likewise what is good for the intellect (`medhāya`) is `mejjhaṃ ghaṭaṃ`; what is good for the feet (`pādānaṃ`) is `pajjaṃ telaṃ` ("foot-oil"); what is good for a chariot (`rathassa`) is `racchā` ("a carriage road"); what exists in a village (`gāme`) is `gammo` ("rustic"); what exists in a cow (`gave`) is `gabyaṃ` ("bovine"), since by the word `ca` in the rule [§78 (31): O sare ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/1-pathamakanda#m78) the replacement by `ava` takes place before the suffix `ya` as well; what exists in a poet (`kavimhi`) is `kabyaṃ` ("poetry"); what exists in the sky (`divi`) is `dibyā` ("divine"); what is born of the breast (`thanato`) is `thaññaṃ` ("mother's milk"); what conduces to wealth (`dhanāya`) is `dhaññaṃ` ("grain").

> Ṇya ppaccayo parisāyaṃ sādhu pārisajjo, da kārāgamo, samaṇānaṃ hitā sāmaññā janā, brāhmaṇānaṃ hitā brāhmaññā, arūpe bhavā āruppā iccādi.

With the suffix `ṇya`: one good in an assembly (`parisāyaṃ`) is `pārisajjo` ("a councillor"), with the insertion of the letter `d`; people good for ascetics (`samaṇānaṃ`) are `sāmaññā janā`; those good for brahmins (`brāhmaṇānaṃ`) are `brāhmaññā`; [states] existing in the formless (`arūpe`) are `āruppā`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | pāda + naṃ (⑥⨂) + ya            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pāda + ~~naṃ~~ + ya             | 332 (§317) |
