| →   | pād + (~~a~~ + ya)              | 369 (§261) |
| →   | pā + (dy→j) + a                 | 41 (§269) |
| →   | pā + (j→jj) + a                 | 40 (§28) |
| →   | p + (ā→a) + jja                 | 354 (§403) |
| →   | pajja + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pajja + (si→aṃ)                 | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **pajjaṃ**                      |      |

|     | go + smiṃ (⑦⨀) + ya             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | go + ~~smiṃ~~ + ya              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | g + (o→ava) + ya                | 31 (§78) |
| →   | ga + (v→b) + ya                 | 370 (§404) |
| →   | gabya + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | gabya + (si→aṃ)                 | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **gabyaṃ**                      |      |

|     | parisā + yaṃ (⑦⨀) + ṇya         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | parisā + ~~yaṃ~~ + ṇya          | 332 (§317), 333 (§318) |
| →   | parisā + (~~ṇ~~ya)              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | p + (a→ā) + risā + ya           | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | pāris + (~~ā~~ + ya)            | 369 (§261) |
| →   | pārisa + (d) + ya               | 370 (§404) |
| →   | pārisa + (dy→j) + a             | 41 (§269) |
| →   | pārisa + (j→jj) + a             | 40 (§28) |
| →   | pārisajja + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pārisajja + (si→o)              | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **pārisajjo**                   |      |

:::

> ‘‘Rājaputtānaṃ samūho’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `rājaputtānaṃ samūho` ("a collection of princes") —

[^6]: CST4 prints `vuttiko`; `puttiko` is read with Kaccāyana's vutti under §353.

[^7]: CST4 prints `ukārādi`, which yields no sense; the Pāḷi is left as printed.

---

<a name="r379"></a>

### 379 (§354): Samūhatthe kaṇaṇā (`kaṇ`, `ṇa` in the sense of a collection) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§354 (379): Samūhatthe kaṇa ṇā" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m354" description="`kaṇ`, `ṇa` in the sense of a collection" />
:::

> Chaṭṭhiyantato ‘‘tesaṃ samūho’’ti atthe kaṇaṇa iccete paccayā honti. Rājaputtako, rājaputtakaṃ vā, rājaputto. Evaṃ mānussako, mānusso, māthurako, māthuro, porisako, poriso, vuddhānaṃ samūho vuddhako, vuddho. Evaṃ māyūrako, māyūro, kāpoto, kokilo, māhiṃsako, māhiṃso, oṭṭhako, orabbhako, aṭṭhannaṃ samūho aṭṭhako, rājānaṃ samūho rājako, bhikkhānaṃ samūho bhikkho, sikkhānaṃ samūho sikkho, dvinnaṃ samūho dvayaṃ, ‘‘tesu vuddhī’’tiādinā i kārassa ayā deso. Evaṃ tiṇṇaṃ samūho tayaṃ iccādi.

After a word ending in the sixth form ⑥, the suffixes `kaṇ` and `ṇa` are used in the sense "a collection of them" (`tesaṃ samūho`): `rājaputtako`, or `rājaputtakaṃ`, and `rājaputto`. Likewise `mānussako` and `mānusso` ("a collection of men"), `māthurako` and `māthuro` ("a collection of Mathurā men"), `porisako` and `poriso` ("a collection of men"); a collection of elders (`vuddhānaṃ`) is `vuddhako` or `vuddho`. Likewise `māyūrako` and `māyūro` ("a flock of peacocks"), `kāpoto` ("a flock of pigeons"), `kokilo` ("a flock of cuckoos"), `māhiṃsako` and `māhiṃso` ("a herd of buffaloes"), `oṭṭhako` ("a herd of camels"), `orabbhako` ("a flock of sheep"); a collection of eight (`aṭṭhannaṃ`) is `aṭṭhako`; a collection of kings (`rājānaṃ`) is `rājako`; a collection of alms (`bhikkhānaṃ`) is `bhikkho`; a collection of trainings (`sikkhānaṃ`) is `sikkho`; a collection of two (`dvinnaṃ`) is `dvayaṃ`, where the letter `i` is replaced by `aya` by [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404) and the rest; likewise a collection of three (`tiṇṇaṃ`) is `tayaṃ`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | manussa + naṃ (⑥⨂) + kaṇ        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | manussa + ~~naṃ~~ + kaṇ         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | manussa + (ka~~ṇ~~)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | m + (a→ā) + nussa + ka          | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | mānussaka + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | mānussaka + (si→o)              | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **mānussako**                   |      |

|     | purisa + naṃ (⑥⨂) + ṇa          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + ~~naṃ~~ + ṇa           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | purisa + (~~ṇ~~a)               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | p + (u→o) + risa + a            | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | poris + (~~a~~ + a)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | porisa + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | porisa + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **poriso**                      |      |

|     | dvi + nnaṃ (⑥⨂) + ṇa            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dvi + ~~nnaṃ~~ + ṇa             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | dvi + (~~ṇ~~a)                  | 363 (§396) |
| →   | dv + (i→aya) + a                | 370 (§404) |
| →   | dvay + (~~a~~ + a)              | 67 (§83) |
| →   | dvaya + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | dvaya + (si→aṃ)                 | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **dvayaṃ**                      |      |

:::

> ‘‘Samūhatthe’’ti vattate.

“`samūhatthe`” ("in the sense of a collection") continues.

---

<a name="r380"></a>

### 380 (§355): Gāmajanabandhusahāyādīhi tā (`tā` after `gāma`, `jana`, `bandhu`, `sahāya` and the like [for a collection]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§355 (380): Gāma jana bandhu sahāyādīhitā" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m355" description="`tā` after `gāma`, `jana`, `bandhu`, `sahāya` and the like [for a collection]" />
:::

> Gāmajanabandhusahāya iccevamādīhi tā paccayo hoti samūhatthe. Gāmānaṃ samūho gāmatā. Evaṃ janatā, bandhutā, sahāyatā, nāgaratā. ‘‘Tā’’ti yogavibhāgena sakatthepi devoyeva devatā, tā paccayantassa niccamitthiliṅgatā.

After `gāma`, `jana`, `bandhu`, `sahāya` and the like, the suffix `tā` is used in the sense of a collection: a collection of villages (`gāmānaṃ`) is `gāmatā`; likewise `janatā` ("a body of people"), `bandhutā` ("a body of kinsmen"), `sahāyatā` ("a company of friends") and `nāgaratā` ("a body of townsmen"). By splitting the rule (`yogavibhāga`) at `tā`, [the suffix is used] also in the word's own sense (`sakattha`): `devatā` is simply `devo` ("a deity"). A word ending in the suffix `tā` is always feminine.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | gāma + naṃ (⑥⨂) + tā            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | gāma + ~~naṃ~~ + tā             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | gāmatā + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | gāmatā + ~~si~~                 | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **gāmatā**                      |      |

:::

---

<a name="r381"></a>

### 381 (§356): Tadassa ṭhānamiyo ca (And `iya` in the sense "that is the occasion of it") :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§356 (381): Tadassa ṭhānamiyo ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m356" description="And `iya` in the sense 'that is the occasion of it'" />
:::

> ‘‘Tadassa ṭhāna’’miccetasmiṃ atthe chaṭṭhiyantato iya ppaccayo hoti. Madanassa ṭhānaṃ madaniyo, madaniyā, madaniyaṃ, bandhanassa ṭhānaṃ bandhaniyaṃ. Evaṃ mucchaniyaṃ. Rajaniyaṃ, gamaniyaṃ, dassaniyaṃ, upādāniyaṃ, pasādaniyaṃ. Ca saddena hitā diatthepi upādānānaṃ hitā upādāniyā iccādi.

After a word ending in the sixth form ⑥, the suffix `iya` is used in the sense "that is the occasion (`ṭhāna`) of it" (`tadassa ṭhānaṃ`): an occasion of intoxication (`madanassa`) is `madaniyo` ("intoxicating"), `madaniyā`, `madaniyaṃ`; an occasion of bondage (`bandhanassa`) is `bandhaniyaṃ` ("captivating"). Likewise `mucchaniyaṃ` ("an occasion of infatuation"), `rajaniyaṃ` ("an occasion of passion"), `gamaniyaṃ` ("an occasion of going"), `dassaniyaṃ` ("an occasion of seeing"), `upādāniyaṃ` ("an occasion of grasping") and `pasādaniyaṃ` ("an occasion of confidence"). By the word `ca`, [the suffix is used] also in the sense "good for" and the like: [states] good for the graspings (`upādānānaṃ`) are `upādāniyā`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | madana + ssa (⑥⨀) + iya         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | madana + ~~ssa~~ + iya          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | madan + (~~a~~ + iya)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | madaniya + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | madaniya + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **madaniyo**                    |      |

:::

---

<a name="r382"></a>

### 382 (§357): Upamatthāyitattaṃ (`āyitatta` in the sense of comparison) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§357 (382): Upamatthāyitattaṃ" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m357" description="`āyitatta` in the sense of comparison" />
:::

> Upamatthe upamāvāciliṅgato āyitatta ppaccayo hoti. Dhūmo viya dissatīti dhūmāyitattaṃ. Evaṃ timirāyitattaṃ.

In the sense of comparison (`upamā`), after a lemma expressing the comparison, the suffix `āyitatta` is used: "it appears like smoke" (`dhūmo viya dissati`), `dhūmāyitattaṃ` ("a smoke-like state"); likewise `timirāyitattaṃ` ("a darkness-like state").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | dhūma + āyitatta                | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dhūm + (~~a~~ + āyitatta)       | 67 (§83) |
| →   | dhūmāyitatta + si               | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | dhūmāyitatta + (si→aṃ)          | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **dhūmāyitattaṃ**               |      |

:::

> ‘‘Tadassa ṭhāna’’nti vattate.

“`tadassa ṭhāna`” ("that is the occasion of it") continues.

---

<a name="r383"></a>

### 383 (§358): Tannissitatthe lo (`la` in the sense "resting on that" [and "the occasion of that"]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§358 (383): Tannissitatthe lo" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m358" description="`la` in the sense 'resting on that' [and 'the occasion of that']" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

“`tadassa ṭhāna`” continues from [381 (§356): Tadassa ṭhānamiyo ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r381), across rule 382.

:::

> ‘‘Tannissita’’nti atthe, ‘‘tadassaṭhāna’’nti atthe ca la ppaccayo hoti. Duṭṭhu nissitaṃ, duṭṭhu ṭhānaṃ vā duṭṭhullaṃ, duṭṭhullā vācā, la ssa dvibhāvo. Evaṃ vedallaṃ.

The suffix `la` is used in the sense "resting on that" (`tannissita`) and in the sense "that is the occasion of it" (`tadassa ṭhāna`): what rests on what is bad (`duṭṭhu`), or is an occasion of what is bad, is `duṭṭhullaṃ` ("coarse"); `duṭṭhullā vācā` ("coarse speech"), with the doubling of the `l`. Likewise `vedallaṃ` ("the catechism").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | duṭṭhu + la                     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | duṭṭhu + (l) + la               | 370 (§404) |
| →   | duṭṭhulla + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | duṭṭhulla + (si→aṃ)             | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **duṭṭhullaṃ**                  |      |

:::

> ‘‘Abhijjhā assa pakati, abhijjhā assa bahulā’’ti vā viggahe –

In the analysis `abhijjhā assa pakati` ("covetousness is his nature"), or `abhijjhā assa bahulā` ("covetousness abounds in him") —

---

<a name="r384"></a>

### 384 (§359): Ālu tabbahule (`ālu` in the sense "abounding in that") :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§359 (384): Ālu tabbahule" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m359" description="`ālu` in the sense 'abounding in that'" />
:::

> Paṭhamāvibhatyantato ālu ppaccayo hoti ‘‘tadassa bahula’’miccetasmiṃ atthe. Abhijjhālu, abhijjhālū, abhijjhālavo. Evaṃ sītālu, dhajālu, dayālu. ‘‘Sabbato ko’’ti ettha puna sabbato ggahaṇena ka kārāgamo, abhijjhāluko, abhijjhālukā, abhijjhālukaṃ. Evaṃ sītāluko, dayāluko, tathā hīnova hīnako. Evaṃ potako, kumārako, māṇavako, muduko, ujuko, appamattakaṃ, oramattakaṃ, sīlamattakaṃ iccādi.

After a word ending in the first inflection ①, the suffix `ālu` is used in the sense "that abounds in him" (`tadassa bahulaṃ`): `abhijjhālu` ("covetous"), `abhijjhālū`, `abhijjhālavo`; likewise `sītālu` ("sensitive to cold"), `dhajālu` ("full of banners") and `dayālu` ("compassionate"). Because `sabbato` is mentioned again in [§178 (224): Sabbato ko](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/3-tatiyakanda#m178), the insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) of the letter `ka` takes place: `abhijjhāluko`, `abhijjhālukā`, `abhijjhālukaṃ`; likewise `sītāluko` and `dayāluko`, and so too `hīnako` is simply `hīno` ("low"). Likewise `potako` ("a young one"), `kumārako` ("a boy"), `māṇavako` ("a youth"), `muduko` ("soft"), `ujuko` ("straight"), `appamattakaṃ` ("a trifle"), `oramattakaṃ` ("a trifle"), `sīlamattakaṃ` ("mere virtue"), and so on.

> ‘‘Yadanupapannā nipātanā sijjhantī’’ti iminā paṭibhāgakucchitasaññānukampādiatthesu ka ppaccayo. Paṭibhāgatthe hatthino iva hatthikā. Evaṃ assakā. Kucchitatthe kucchito samaṇo samaṇako. Evaṃ brāhmaṇako, muṇḍako, paṇḍitako, veyyākaraṇako. Saññāyaṃ katako, bhaṭako. Anukampāyaṃ puttako.

By [§391 (423): Yadanupapannā nipātanā sijjhanti](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m391), the suffix `ka` [is used] in the senses of resemblance (`paṭibhāga`), contempt (`kucchita`), a name (`saññā`), compassion (`anukampā`) and the like. In the sense of resemblance, [things] like elephants (`hatthino iva`) are `hatthikā` ("toy elephants"); likewise `assakā` ("toy horses"). In the sense of contempt, a despicable ascetic (`kucchito samaṇo`) is `samaṇako`; likewise `brāhmaṇako` ("a wretched brahmin"), `muṇḍako` ("a shaveling"), `paṇḍitako` ("a would-be scholar") and `veyyākaraṇako` ("a would-be grammarian"). As a name: `katako`, `bhaṭako`. In compassion: `puttako` ("little son").

> Tathā kiṃyateta to parimāṇatthe ttakavantu ppaccayā. Kiṃ parimāṇamassāti kittakaṃ. Evaṃ yattakaṃ, tattakaṃ, ettakaṃ. Vantumhi ā ttañca, yaṃ parimāṇamassāti yāvā, yāvanto, guṇavantusamaṃ. Evaṃ tāvā, tāvanto. Etāvā, etāvanto iccādi.

So too, after `kiṃ`, `ya`, `ta` and `eta`, the suffixes `ttaka` and `vantu` [are used] in the sense of measure (`parimāṇa`): "what is its measure?" (`kiṃ parimāṇaṃ assa`), `kittakaṃ` ("how much"); likewise `yattakaṃ` ("as much as"), `tattakaṃ` ("so much") and `ettakaṃ` ("this much"). Before `vantu` there is also the change to `ā`: "its measure is that which" (`yaṃ parimāṇaṃ assa`), `yāvā`, `yāvanto` ("as much as"), like `guṇavantu`; likewise `tāvā`, `tāvanto` ("so much"), `etāvā`, `etāvanto` ("this much"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | abhijjhā + si (①⨀) + ālu        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | abhijjhā + ~~si~~ + ālu         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | abhijjh + (~~ā~~ + ālu)         | 67 (§83) |
| →   | abhijjhālu + si                 | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | abhijjhālu + ~~si~~             | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **abhijjhālu**                  |      |

|     | abhijjhālu + si (①⨀)            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | abhijjhālu + (ka) + si          | 224 (§178) |
| →   | abhijjhāluka + (si→o)           | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **abhijjhāluko**                |      |

|     | kiṃ + ttaka                     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ki + ~~ṃ~~ + ttaka              | 423 (§391) |
| →   | kittaka + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kittaka + (si→aṃ)               | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **kittakaṃ**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Suvaṇṇena pakata’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis `suvaṇṇena pakataṃ` ("made of gold") —

---

<a name="r385"></a>

### 385 (§372): Tappakativacane mayo (`maya` in the sense "made of that") :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§372 (385): Tappakativacane mayo" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m372" description="`maya` in the sense 'made of that'" />
:::

> Tappakativacanatthe maya ppaccayo hoti, pakarīyatīti pakati, tena pakati tappakati, tappakatiyā vacanaṃ kathanaṃ tappakativacanaṃ. Suvaṇṇamayo ratho, sovaṇṇamayo vā, suvaṇṇamayā bhājanavikati, suvaṇṇamayaṃ bhājanaṃ. Evaṃ rūpiyamayaṃ, rajatamayaṃ, jatumayaṃ, dārumayaṃ, mattikāmayaṃ, iddhiyā nibbattaṃ iddhimayaṃ.

The suffix `maya` is used in the sense of stating that as the material (`tappakativacana`): the material (`pakati`) is so called because it is made (`pakarīyati`); the material [consisting] of that (`tena pakati`) is `tappakati`; and the statement (`vacana`), that is, the telling (`kathana`), of `tappakati` is `tappakativacana`. A chariot made of gold is `suvaṇṇamayo ratho`, or `sovaṇṇamayo`; `suvaṇṇamayā bhājanavikati` ("a worked vessel of gold"); `suvaṇṇamayaṃ bhājanaṃ` ("a golden vessel"). Likewise `rūpiyamayaṃ` ("made of silver"), `rajatamayaṃ` ("made of silver"), `jatumayaṃ` ("made of lac"), `dārumayaṃ` ("made of wood") and `mattikāmayaṃ` ("made of clay"); what is produced by psychic power (`iddhiyā`) is `iddhimayaṃ`.

> Manato nipphannā manomayā, ayasāpakataṃ ayomayaṃ. Ettha ca ‘‘manogaṇādīna’’nti vattamāne –

[Things] produced from mind (`manato`) are `manomayā` ("mind-made"); what is made of iron (`ayasā`) is `ayomayaṃ`. And here, while “`manogaṇādīnaṃ`” ("of the `mano`-group and the like") continues from [§181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/3-tatiyakanda#m181) —

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | suvaṇṇa + nā (③⨀) + maya        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | suvaṇṇa + ~~nā~~ + maya         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | suvaṇṇamaya + si                | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | suvaṇṇamaya + (si→o)            | 66 (§104) |
| →   | s + (u→o) + vaṇṇamayo           | 423 (§391) (optional) |
| →   | **suvaṇṇamayo**, **sovaṇṇamayo** |      |

:::

---

<a name="r386"></a>

### 386 (§183): Etesamo lope (`o` for the final of these when the inflection is elided) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§183 (48): Etesamo lope" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/3-tatiyakanda#m183" description="`o` for the final of these when the inflection is elided" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

This sutta was already stated in the Sandhikaṇḍa as [48 (§183): Etesamo lope](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r48); the Kaccāyana card above keeps that number.

:::

> Etesaṃ manogaṇādīnaṃ anto o ttamāpajjate vibhattilope kateti o kāro.

The final of these, the `mano`-group and the like, becomes `o` once the inflection has been elided; hence the letter `o`.

> Gavena pakataṃ karīsaṃ, goto nibbattanti vā gomayaṃ. ‘‘Mayo’’tiyogavibhāgena sakatthepi dānameva dānamayaṃ, sīlamayaṃ iccādi.

Dung (`karīsaṃ`) made by a cow (`gavena`), or produced from a cow (`goto`), is `gomayaṃ`. By splitting the rule (`yogavibhāga`) at `mayo`, [the suffix is used] also in the word's own sense (`sakattha`): `dānamayaṃ` is simply `dānaṃ` ("giving"); so too `sīlamayaṃ` ("virtue"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | go + nā (③⨀) + maya             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | go + ~~nā~~ + maya              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | gomaya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | gomaya + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **gomayaṃ**                     |      |

:::

---

> _Saṃsaṭṭhādianekatthataddhitaṃ._

[Here ends] the `taddhita` [section] of "mixed with" and the rest, and of the many senses.

---

## Bhāvataddhita (Abstract suffixes)

> ‘‘Alasassa bhāvo’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `alasassa bhāvo` ("the state of a lazy man") —

---

<a name="r387"></a>

### 387 (§360): Ṇyattatā bhāve tu (`ṇya`, `tta`, `tā` in the sense of the abstract state; and `ttana`) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§360 (387): Ṇya tta tā bhāve tu" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m360" description="`ṇya`, `tta`, `tā` in the sense of the abstract state; and `ttana`" />
:::

> Chaṭṭhiyantato ṇyattatā iccete paccayā honti ‘‘tassa bhāvo’’ iccetasmiṃ atthe, tu saddaggahaṇena ttanaṇeyyā dippaccayā ca. Bhavanti etasmā buddhisaddā iti bhāvo, saddappavattinimittaṃ vuccati, vuttañca – ‘‘yassa guṇassa hi bhāvā dabbe saddaniveso tadabhidhāneṇyattatādayo’’ti. Ṇyattattana ntānaṃ niccaṃ napuṃsakattaṃ, tā paccayantassa sabhāvato niccamitthiliṅgatā. Ṇya ppaccayoyaṃ guṇavacane brāhmaṇā dīhi, tattha ‘‘avaṇṇo ye lopañcā’’ti avaṇṇa lopo, ādivuddhi.

After a word ending in the sixth form ⑥, the suffixes `ṇya`, `tta` and `tā` are used in the sense "the state of that" (`tassa bhāvo`), and by the mention of the word `tu` the suffixes `ttana`, `ṇeyya` and the rest as well. The abstract state (`bhāva`) is that from which the idea and the word arise (`bhavanti etasmā buddhisaddā`); it is called the ground for the application of a word (`saddappavattinimitta`), and it is said: "It is because of the presence (`bhāva`) of a quality in a thing that a word is applied to the thing; `ṇya`, `tta`, `tā` and the rest express that [quality]." A word ending in `ṇya`, `tta` or `ttana` is always neuter, while a word ending in the suffix `tā` is by its nature always feminine. This suffix `ṇya` comes after words expressing a quality and after `brāhmaṇa` and the like; there the `a`-vowel is elided by [§261 (369): Avaṇṇo ye lopañca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m261), and the first vowel takes `vuddhi`.

> Ālasyaṃ. Evaṃ ārogyaṃ, udaggassa bhāvo odagyaṃ, sakhino bhāvo sakhyaṃ, aṇaṇassa bhāvo āṇaṇyaṃ, vidhavāya bhāvo vedhabyaṃ, dubbalassa bhāvo dubbalyaṃ, capalassa bhāvo cāpalyaṃ.

`ālasyaṃ` ("laziness"). Likewise `ārogyaṃ` ("health"); the state of one who is elated (`udaggassa bhāvo`) is `odagyaṃ`; the state of a friend (`sakhino bhāvo`) is `sakhyaṃ`; the state of one free of debt (`aṇaṇassa bhāvo`) is `āṇaṇyaṃ`; the state of a widow (`vidhavāya bhāvo`) is `vedhabyaṃ`; the state of a weak man (`dubbalassa bhāvo`) is `dubbalyaṃ`; the state of a fickle man (`capalassa bhāvo`) is `cāpalyaṃ`.

:::tip[Derivation]

| →   | ālasya + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **ālasyaṃ**                     |      |

| →   | odagg + (~~a~~ + ya)            | 369 (§261) |
| →   | odagya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |

:::

> Viyattassa bhāvo veyyattiyaṃ, maccharassa bhāvo macchariyaṃ. Evaṃ issariyaṃ, ālasiyaṃ, muṇḍiyaṃ, mūḷhiyaṃ. Ettha ‘‘veyyattiya’’ntiādīsu ‘‘tesu vuddhī’’tiādinā ya mhi i kārāgamo.

The state of one who is accomplished (`viyattassa bhāvo`) is `veyyattiyaṃ`; the state of a selfish man (`maccharassa bhāvo`) is `macchariyaṃ`. Likewise `issariyaṃ` ("lordship"), `ālasiyaṃ` ("laziness"), `muṇḍiyaṃ` ("the shaven state") and `mūḷhiyaṃ` ("bewilderment"). Here, in `veyyattiyaṃ` and the rest, the insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) of the letter `i` before the `ya` comes by [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404) and the rest.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | viyatta + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇya         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | viyatta + ~~sa~~ + ṇya          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | viyatta + (~~ṇ~~)ya             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v(i→e)yatta + ya                | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | ve + (y)yatta + ya              | 40 (§28) |
| →   | veyyatt + (~~a~~ + ya)          | 369 (§261) |
| →   | veyyatt + (i) + ya              | 370 (§404) |
| →   | veyyattiya + si                 | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | veyyattiya + (si→aṃ)            | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **veyyattiyaṃ**                 |      |

:::

> ‘‘Paṇḍitassa bhāvo paṇḍitya’’ntiādīsu ‘‘yavataṃ talanadakārānaṃ byañjanāni calañajakāratta’’nti tya kārasaṃyogādīnaṃ calañaja kārādesā, dvittaṃ. Paṇḍiccaṃ, bahussutassa bhāvo bāhussaccaṃ, ‘‘tesu vuddhī’’tiādinā u kārassa a kāro, evaṃ porohiccaṃ, adhipatissa bhāvo ādhipaccaṃ, muṭṭhassatissa bhāvo muṭṭhassaccaṃ, ivaṇṇa lopo. Kusalassa bhāvo kosallaṃ. Evaṃ vepullaṃ, samānānaṃ bhāvo sāmaññaṃ, gilānassa bhāvo gelaññaṃ, ‘‘kvacādimajjhuttarā’’disuttena saṃyoge pare rassattaṃ.

In `paṇḍitassa bhāvo paṇḍitya` ("the state of a learned man") and the like, the conjunct `ty` and the rest are replaced by the letters `c`, `l`, `ñ` and `j` by [§269 (41): Yavataṃ ta la ṇa dakārānaṃ byañjanāni ca la ña jakārattaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m269), and there is doubling: `paṇḍiccaṃ` ("learning"). The state of one who has heard much (`bahussutassa bhāvo`) is `bāhussaccaṃ`, where the letter `u` becomes `a` by §404 and the rest; likewise `porohiccaṃ` ("the office of chaplain"); the state of a lord (`adhipatissa bhāvo`) is `ādhipaccaṃ`; the state of one whose mindfulness is lost (`muṭṭhassatissa bhāvo`) is `muṭṭhassaccaṃ`, with elision of the `i`-vowel. The state of one who is skilled (`kusalassa bhāvo`) is `kosallaṃ`; likewise `vepullaṃ` ("abundance"); the state of equals (`samānānaṃ bhāvo`) is `sāmaññaṃ`; the state of a sick man (`gilānassa bhāvo`) is `gelaññaṃ`, with shortening before the conjunct by the rule [§403 (354): Kvacādimajjhuttarānaṃ dīgharassā paccayesu ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m403) and the rest.

:::tip[Derivation]

> Suhadassa bhāvosohajjaṃ. Evaṃ vesārajjaṃ, kusīdassabhāvokosajjaṃ, ‘‘tesu vuddhī’’tiādinā īkārassa a kāro. Tathā ‘‘purisassa bhāvo porisa’’ntiādīsu ‘‘yavataṃ talanā’’disutte kāra ggahaṇena yavataṃ sa kārakacaṭapa vaggānaṃ sa kārakacaṭapa vaggādesā. Sumanassa bhāvo somanassaṃ. Evaṃ domanassaṃ, sovacassaṃ, dovacassaṃ, ettha sa kārāgamo. Tathā nipakassa bhāvo nepakkaṃ, dvittaṃ. Evaṃ ādhikkaṃ, dubhagassa bhāvo dobhaggaṃ, vāṇijassa bhāvo vāṇijjaṃ, rājino bhāvo rajjaṃ, ‘‘kvacā’’dinā rassattaṃ. Sarūpassa bhāvo sāruppaṃ. Evaṃ opammaṃ, sokhummaṃ.

The state of a good-hearted man (`suhadassa bhāvo`) is `sohajjaṃ`; likewise `vesārajjaṃ` ("confidence"); the state of an idle man (`kusīdassa bhāvo`) is `kosajjaṃ`, where the letter `ī` becomes `a` by §404 and the rest. So too, in `purisassa bhāvo porisaṃ` ("the state of a man") and the like, by the mention of the word `kāra` in the rule §269, an `s` or a consonant of the `k`, `c`, `ṭ` or `p` class that carries a `y` is replaced by that `s` or by a consonant of the same class. The state of one of good mind (`sumanassa bhāvo`) is `somanassaṃ`; likewise `domanassaṃ` ("dejection"), `sovacassaṃ` ("compliance") and `dovacassaṃ` ("obstinacy"), where the letter `s` is inserted. So too the state of a prudent man (`nipakassa bhāvo`) is `nepakkaṃ`, with doubling; likewise `ādhikkaṃ` ("excess"); the state of an unfortunate man (`dubhagassa bhāvo`) is `dobhaggaṃ`; the state of a merchant (`vāṇijassa bhāvo`) is `vāṇijjaṃ`; the state of a king (`rājino bhāvo`) is `rajjaṃ`, with shortening by §403 and the rest. The state of what is of like form (`sarūpassa bhāvo`) is `sāruppaṃ`; likewise `opammaṃ` ("comparison") and `sokhummaṃ` ("subtlety").

:::tip[Derivation]

| →   | somanassa + (si→aṃ)             | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **somanassaṃ**                  |      |

| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | nipaka + ~~sa~~ + ṇya           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | nipaka + (~~ṇ~~)ya              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | n(i→e)paka + ya                 | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | nepak + (~~a~~ + ya)            | 369 (§261) |
| →   | nepa + (ky→k) + a               | 41 (§269) |
| →   | nepa + (k→kk) + a               | 40 (§28) |
| →   | nepakka + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | nepakka + (si→aṃ)               | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **nepakkaṃ**                    |      |

|     | rāja + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇya            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | rāja + ~~sa~~ + ṇya             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | rāja + (~~ṇ~~)ya                | 363 (§396) |
| →   | rāj + (~~a~~ + ya)              | 369 (§261) |
| →   | rā + (jy→j) + a                 | 41 (§269) |
| →   | rā + (j→jj) + a                 | 40 (§28) |
| →   | r(ā→a)jja                       | 354 (§403) |
| →   | rajja + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | rajja + (si→aṃ)                 | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **rajjaṃ**                      |      |

:::

> Tathassa bhāvo tacchaṃ, dummedhassa bhāvo dummejjhaṃ, samaṇassa bhāvo sāmaññaṃ. Evaṃ brāhmaññaṃ, nipuṇassa bhāvo nepuññaṃ. ‘‘Taccha’’ntiādīsupi kāra ggahaṇeneva yavataṃ thadhaṇa kārānaṃ chajhaña kārādesā.

The state of what is so (`tathassa bhāvo`) is `tacchaṃ`; the state of a dull-witted man (`dummedhassa bhāvo`) is `dummejjhaṃ`; the state of an ascetic (`samaṇassa bhāvo`) is `sāmaññaṃ`; likewise `brāhmaññaṃ` ("the brahmin state"); the state of a skilful man (`nipuṇassa bhāvo`) is `nepuññaṃ`. In `tacchaṃ` and the rest too, it is only by the mention of the word `kāra` that the letters `th`, `dh` and `ṇ` carrying a `y` are replaced by the letters `ch`, `jh` and `ñ`.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | tatha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇya           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tatha + ~~sa~~ + ṇya            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | tatha + (~~ṇ~~)ya               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | tath + (~~a~~ + ya)             | 369 (§261) |
| →   | ta + (thy→ch) + a               | 41 (§269) |
| →   | ta + (ch→cch) + a               | 42 (§29) |
| →   | taccha + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | taccha + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **tacchaṃ**                     |      |

|     | samaṇa + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇya          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | samaṇa + ~~sa~~ + ṇya           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | samaṇa + (~~ṇ~~)ya              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | s(a→ā)maṇa + ya                 | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | sāmaṇ + (~~a~~ + ya)            | 369 (§261) |
| →   | sāma + (ṇy→ñ) + a               | 41 (§269) |
| →   | sāma + (ñ→ññ) + a               | 40 (§28) |
| →   | sāmañña + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | sāmañña + (si→aṃ)               | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **sāmaññaṃ**                    |      |

:::

> Ttatā paccayesu – paṃsukūlikassa bhāvo paṃsukūlikattaṃ, paṃsukūlikatā. Evaṃ tecīvarikattaṃ, tecīvarikatā, odarikattaṃ, odarikatā, manussattaṃ, manussatā jāti, nīlattaṃ, nīlatā guṇo, yācakattaṃ, yācakatā kriyā, daṇḍittaṃ, daṇḍitā dabbaṃ, saccavāditā, pāramitā, kataññutā, sabbaññutā, ‘‘kvacā’’dinā tā paccaye rassattaṃ, appicchatā, asaṃsaggatā, bhassārāmatā, niddārāmatā, lahutā iccādi.

With the suffixes `tta` and `tā`: the state of a wearer of rag robes (`paṃsukūlikassa bhāvo`) is `paṃsukūlikattaṃ`, `paṃsukūlikatā`. Likewise `tecīvarikattaṃ`, `tecīvarikatā` ("the state of a wearer of the three robes"); `odarikattaṃ`, `odarikatā` ("gluttony"); `manussattaṃ`, `manussatā` ("humanity") — a kind (`jāti`); `nīlattaṃ`, `nīlatā` ("blueness") — a quality (`guṇa`); `yācakattaṃ`, `yācakatā` ("the state of a beggar") — an action (`kriyā`); `daṇḍittaṃ`, `daṇḍitā` ("the state of a staff-bearer") — a substance (`dabba`); `saccavāditā` ("truthfulness"), `pāramitā` ("perfection"), `kataññutā` ("gratitude"), `sabbaññutā` ("omniscience"), with shortening before the suffix `tā` by [§403 (354): Kvacādimajjhuttarānaṃ dīgharassā paccayesu ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m403) and the rest; `appicchatā` ("fewness of wishes"), `asaṃsaggatā` ("aloofness"), `bhassārāmatā` ("delight in talk"), `niddārāmatā` ("delight in sleep"), `lahutā` ("lightness"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | paṃsukūlika + sa (⑥⨀) + tta     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | paṃsukūlika + ~~sa~~ + tta      | 332 (§317) |
| →   | paṃsukūlikatta + si             | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | paṃsukūlikatta + (si→aṃ)        | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **paṃsukūlikattaṃ**             |      |

|     | manussa + sa (⑥⨀) + tā          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | manussa + ~~sa~~ + tā           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | manussatā + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | manussatā + ~~si~~              | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **manussatā**                   |      |

|     | daṇḍī + sa (⑥⨀) + tā            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | daṇḍī + ~~sa~~ + tā             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | daṇḍ(ī→i)tā                     | 354 (§403) |
| →   | daṇḍitā + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | daṇḍitā + ~~si~~                | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **daṇḍitā**                     |      |

|     | kataññū + sa (⑥⨀) + tā          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kataññū + ~~sa~~ + tā           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | katañ(ñū→ñu)tā                  | 354 (§403) |
| →   | kataññutā + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kataññutā + ~~si~~              | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **kataññutā**                   |      |

:::

:::tip[Examples]

> `kammārāmatā, bhassārāmatā, niddārāmatā, saṅgaṇikārāmatā` \
> [(16A5 2.4.9: Paṭhamasekhasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/16A5/2/2.4/2.4.9#556) \
🚺①⨀(kammārāma + tā) 🚺①⨀(bhassārāma + tā) 🚺①⨀(niddārāma + tā) 🚺①⨀(saṅgaṇikārāma + tā) \
delight in work | delight in talk | delight in sleep | delight in company

> `appicchatā, santuṭṭhitā, sallekhatā` \
> [(5V 7.3: Tikavāra)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/5V/7/7.3#1464) \
🚺①⨀(appiccha + tā) 🚺①⨀(santuṭṭhi + tā) 🚺①⨀(sallekha + tā) \
fewness of wishes | contentment | austerity

:::

> Ttana paccaye – puthujjanassa bhāvo puthujjanattanaṃ, vedanattanaṃ, jāyattanaṃ.

With the suffix `ttana`: the state of an ordinary person (`puthujjanassa bhāvo`) is `puthujjanattanaṃ`; `vedanattanaṃ` ("the state of feeling"); `jāyattanaṃ` ("wifehood").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | puthujjana + sa (⑥⨀) + ttana    | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puthujjana + ~~sa~~ + ttana     | 332 (§317) |
| →   | puthujjanattana + si            | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | puthujjanattana + (si→aṃ)       | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **puthujjanattanaṃ**            |      |

:::

> Ṇeyye – sucissa bhāvo soceyyaṃ. Evaṃ ādhipateyyaṃ[^8], kavissa bhāvokāveyyaṃ, thenassa bhāvo theyyaṃ, mahāvuttinā na kārassa lopo.

[^8]: CST4 prints `ādhipabheyyaṃ`; `ādhipateyyaṃ` is read.

With `ṇeyya`: the state of one who is pure (`sucissa bhāvo`) is `soceyyaṃ`; likewise `ādhipateyyaṃ` ("supremacy"); the state of a poet (`kavissa bhāvo`) is `kāveyyaṃ`; the state of a thief (`thenassa bhāvo`) is `theyyaṃ`, with elision of the letter `n` by the great rule (`mahāvutti`).

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | suci + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇeyya          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | suci + ~~sa~~ + ṇeyya           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | suci + (~~ṇ~~)eyya              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | s(u→o)ci + eyya                 | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | soc + (~~i~~ + eyya)            | 67 (§83) |
| →   | soceyya + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | soceyya + (si→aṃ)               | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **soceyyaṃ**                    |      |

|     | thena + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇeyya         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | thena + ~~sa~~ + ṇeyya          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | thena + (~~ṇ~~)eyya             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | then + (~~a~~ + eyya)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | the + (~~n~~) + eyya            | 370 (§404) |
| →   | theyya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | theyya + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **theyyaṃ**                     |      |

:::

> ‘‘Ṇyattatā’’ti yogavibhāgena kammani, sakatthe ca ṇyā dayo, vīrānaṃ bhāvo, kammaṃ vā vīriyaṃ, paribhaṭassa kammaṃ pāribhaṭyaṃ, pāribhaṭyassa bhāvo pāribhaṭyatā. Evaṃ sovacassatā, bhisaggassa kammaṃ bhesajjaṃ, byāvaṭassa kammaṃ veyyāvaccaṃ, saṭhassa bhāvo, kammaṃ vā sāṭheyyaṃ.

By splitting the rule (`yogavibhāga`) at `Ṇyattatā`, `ṇya` and the rest [are used] also in the sense of the action (`kamma`) and in the word's own sense (`sakattha`): the state, or the action, of heroes (`vīrānaṃ bhāvo, kammaṃ vā`) is `vīriyaṃ` ("energy"); the work of one who is nursed (`paribhaṭassa kammaṃ`) is `pāribhaṭyaṃ` ("nursing"); the state of nursing (`pāribhaṭyassa bhāvo`) is `pāribhaṭyatā`; likewise `sovacassatā` ("compliance"); the work of a physician (`bhisaggassa kammaṃ`) is `bhesajjaṃ` ("medicine"); the work of one who is busy (`byāvaṭassa kammaṃ`) is `veyyāvaccaṃ` ("service"); the state, or the act, of a cheat (`saṭhassa bhāvo, kammaṃ vā`) is `sāṭheyyaṃ` ("fraud").

:::tip[Derivation]

| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vīra + ~~naṃ~~ + ṇya            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | vīra + (~~ṇ~~)ya                | 363 (§396) |
| →   | vīr + (~~a~~ + ya)              | 369 (§261) |
| →   | vīr + (i) + ya                  | 370 (§404) |
| →   | vīriya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vīriya + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **vīriyaṃ**                     |      |

|     | bhisagga + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇya        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bhisagga + ~~sa~~ + ṇya         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | bhisagga + (~~ṇ~~)ya            | 363 (§396) |
| →   | bh(i→e)sagga + ya               | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | bhesa(gg→j) + (~~a~~ + ya)      | 369 (§261), 370 (§404) |
| →   | bhesa + (jy→j) + a              | 41 (§269) |
| →   | bhesa + (j→jj) + a              | 40 (§28) |
| →   | bhesajja + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | bhesajja + (si→aṃ)              | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **bhesajjaṃ**                   |      |

:::

> Sakatthe pana – yathābhūtameva yathābhuccaṃ, karuṇāyeva kāruññaṃ, pattakālameva pattakallaṃ, ākāsānantameva ākāsānañcaṃ, kāyapāguññameva kāyapāguññatā iccādi.

In the word's own sense, however: `yathābhuccaṃ` is simply `yathābhūtaṃ` ("as it really is"); `kāruññaṃ` is simply `karuṇā` ("compassion"); `pattakallaṃ` is simply `pattakālaṃ` ("the fitting time"); `ākāsānañcaṃ` is simply `ākāsānantaṃ` ("the infinity of space"); `kāyapāguññatā` is simply `kāyapāguññaṃ` ("proficiency of the body"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | karuṇā + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇya          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | karuṇā + ~~sa~~ + ṇya           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | karuṇā + (~~ṇ~~)ya              | 363 (§396) |
| →   | k(a→ā)ruṇā + ya                 | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | kāruṇ + (~~ā~~ + ya)            | 369 (§261) |
| →   | kāru + (ṇy→ñ) + a               | 41 (§269) |
| →   | kāru + (ñ→ññ) + a               | 40 (§28) |
| →   | kāruñña + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kāruñña + (si→aṃ)               | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **kāruññaṃ**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Visamassa bhāvo’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `visamassa bhāvo` ("the state of what is uneven") —

> ‘‘Ttatā, bhāve’’ti ca vattate.

“`ttatā`” and “`bhāve`” also continue.

<a name="r388"></a>

### 388 (§361): Ṇa visamādīhi (`ṇa` after `visama` and the like [for the abstract state]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§361 (388): Ṇa visamādīhi" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m361" description="`ṇa` after `visama` and the like [for the abstract state]" />
:::

> Visama iccevamādīhi chaṭṭhiyantehi ṇa ppaccayo hoti, tta tā ca ‘‘tassa bhāvo’’ iccetasmiṃ atthe. Ākatigaṇoyaṃ. Vesamaṃ, visamattaṃ, visamatā. Sucissa bhāvo socaṃ, sucittaṃ, sucitā, garuno bhāvo gāravo, ādivuddhi, ‘‘o sare cā’’ti sutte ca saddaggahaṇena u kārassa ca avā deso. Paṭuno bhāvo pāṭavaṃ, paṭuttaṃ, paṭutā.

After `visama` ("uneven") and the like, in the sixth form ⑥, the suffix `ṇa` is used in the sense "the state of that" (`tassa bhāvo`), and so are `tta` and `tā`. This is a group defined by its form (`ākatigaṇa`). `vesamaṃ`, `visamattaṃ`, `visamatā` ("unevenness"). `sucissa bhāvo` ("the state of one who is pure"): `socaṃ`, `sucittaṃ`, `sucitā` ("purity"); `garuno bhāvo` ("the state of a teacher"): `gāravo` ("respect"), with vuddhi of the first vowel, and with the `u` replaced by `ava` through the mention of the word `ca` in the rule [§78 (31): O sare ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/1-pathamakanda#m78); `paṭuno bhāvo` ("the state of one who is clever"): `pāṭavaṃ`, `paṭuttaṃ`, `paṭutā` ("cleverness").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | visama + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | visama + ~~sa~~ + ṇa            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | visama + (~~ṇ~~)a               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | v(i→e)sama + a                  | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | vesam + (~~a~~ + a)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | vesama + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vesama + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **vesamaṃ**                     |      |

|     | suci + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | suci + ~~sa~~ + ṇa              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | suci + (~~ṇ~~)a                 | 363 (§396) |
| →   | s(u→o)ci + a                    | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | soc + (~~i~~ + a)               | 67 (§83) |
| →   | soca + si                       | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | soca + (si→aṃ)                  | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **socaṃ**                       |      |

|     | garu + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | garu + ~~sa~~ + ṇa              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | garu + (~~ṇ~~)a                 | 363 (§396) |
| →   | g(a→ā)ru + a                    | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | gār(u→ava) + a                  | 31 (§78) |
| →   | gārava + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | gārava + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **gāravo**                      |      |

:::

> Ujuno bhāvo ajjavaṃ, muduno bhāvo maddavaṃ iccatra ‘‘āttañcā’’ti ṇa mhi i kārukārānaṃ ā ttaṃ, dvi bhāvo, saṃyoge ādirassattañca. Ujutā, mudutā. Evaṃ isissa bhāvo ārisyaṃ, āsabhaṃ, kumārassa bhāvokomāraṃ, yuvassa bhāvo yobbanaṃ, mahāvuttinā na kārāgamo, paramānaṃ bhāvo, kammaṃ vā pāramī dānādikriyā, ‘‘ṇavaṇikā’’disuttena ī paccayo, samaggānaṃ bhāvo sāmaggī.

In `ajjavaṃ` ("straightness"), from `ujuno bhāvo` ("the state of what is straight"), and `maddavaṃ` ("softness"), from `muduno bhāvo` ("the state of what is soft"), the letters `i` and `u` become `ā` before `ṇa` by [§402 (377): Āttañca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m402); there is doubling, and the initial vowel is shortened before the conjunct. `ujutā` ("straightness"), `mudutā` ("softness"). In the same way `isissa bhāvo` ("the state of a sage"): `ārisyaṃ`; `āsabhaṃ` ("the state of a bull"); `kumārassa bhāvo` ("the state of a boy"): `komāraṃ` ("boyhood"); `yuvassa bhāvo` ("the state of a young man"): `yobbanaṃ` ("youth"), with the insertion ([`āgama`](/introduction#types-of-vidhi-operation)) of the letter `n` by the great rule (`mahāvutti`); `paramānaṃ bhāvo, kammaṃ vā` ("the state, or the action, of the supreme ones"): `pāramī` ("perfection"), the action of giving and the rest, with the suffix `ī` by the sutta [§239 (190): Ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntuhi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/4-catutthakanda#m239); `samaggānaṃ bhāvo` ("the state of those in concord"): `sāmaggī` ("concord").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | uju + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | uju + ~~sa~~ + ṇa               | 332 (§317) |
| →   | uju + (~~ṇ~~)a                  | 363 (§396) |
| →   | (u→ā)ju + a                     | 377 (§402) |
| →   | āj(u→ava) + a                   | 31 (§78) |
| →   | ā + (j)java                     | 40 (§28) |
| →   | (ā→a)jjava                      | 354 (§403) |
| →   | ajjava + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | ajjava + (si→aṃ)                | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **ajjavaṃ**                     |      |

|     | yuva + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | yuva + ~~sa~~ + ṇa              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | yuva + (~~ṇ~~)a                 | 363 (§396) |
| →   | y(u→o)va + a                    | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | yova + (n) + a                  | 370 (§404) |
| →   | yo(va→bba)na                    | 370 (§404) |
| →   | yobbana + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | yobbana + (si→aṃ)               | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **yobbanaṃ**                    |      |

|     | parama + naṃ (⑥⨂) + ṇa          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | parama + ~~naṃ~~ + ṇa           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | parama + (~~ṇ~~)a               | 363 (§396) |
| →   | p(a→ā)rama + a                  | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | pāram + (~~a~~ + a)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | pārama + ī                      | 190 (§239) |
| →   | pāram + (~~a~~ + ī)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | pāramī + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pāramī + ~~si~~                 | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **pāramī**                      |      |

:::

<a name="r389"></a>

### 389 (§362): Ramaṇīyādito kaṇa (`kaṇ` after `ramaṇīya` and the like [for the abstract state]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§362 (389): Ramaṇīyādito kaṇa" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m362" description="`kaṇ` after `ramaṇīya` and the like [for the abstract state]" />
:::

> Ramaṇīya iccevamādito kaṇa paccayo hoti, tta tā ca bhāvatthe. Ramaṇīyassa bhāvo rāmaṇīyakaṃ, ramaṇīyattaṃ, ramaṇīyatā. Evaṃ mānuññakaṃ, manuññattaṃ, manuññatā, piyarūpakaṃ, piyarūpattaṃ, piyarūpatā, kalyāṇakaṃ, kalyāṇattaṃ, kalyāṇatā, corakaṃ, corikā vā, corattaṃ, coratā, aḍḍhakaṃ, aḍḍhattaṃ, aḍḍhatā iccādi.

After `ramaṇīya` ("delightful") and the like the suffix `kaṇ` is used in the sense of the abstract state (`bhāva`), and so are `tta` and `tā`. `ramaṇīyassa bhāvo` ("the state of what is delightful"): `rāmaṇīyakaṃ`, `ramaṇīyattaṃ`, `ramaṇīyatā` ("delightfulness"). In the same way `mānuññakaṃ`, `manuññattaṃ`, `manuññatā` ("pleasantness"); `piyarūpakaṃ`, `piyarūpattaṃ`, `piyarūpatā` ("agreeableness"); `kalyāṇakaṃ`, `kalyāṇattaṃ`, `kalyāṇatā` ("goodness"); `corakaṃ` ("thievery"), or `corikā` ("theft"), `corattaṃ`, `coratā` ("the state of a thief"); `aḍḍhakaṃ`, `aḍḍhattaṃ`, `aḍḍhatā` ("wealthiness"); and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | ramaṇīya + sa (⑥⨀) + kaṇ        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ramaṇīya + ~~sa~~ + kaṇ         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | ramaṇīya + ka(~~ṇ~~)            | 363 (§396) |
| →   | r(a→ā)maṇīya + ka               | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | rāmaṇīyaka + si                 | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | rāmaṇīyaka + (si→aṃ)            | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **rāmaṇīyakaṃ**                 |      |

|     | manuñña + sa (⑥⨀) + kaṇ         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | manuñña + ~~sa~~ + kaṇ          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | manuñña + ka(~~ṇ~~)             | 363 (§396) |
| →   | m(a→ā)nuñña + ka                | 364 (§400), 365 (§405) |
| →   | mānuññaka + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | mānuññaka + (si→aṃ)             | 195 (§219) |
| →   | **mānuññakaṃ**                  |      |

|     | ramaṇīya + sa (⑥⨀) + tā         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ramaṇīya + ~~sa~~ + tā          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | ramaṇīyatā + si                 | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | ramaṇīyatā + ~~si~~             | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **ramaṇīyatā**                  |      |

:::

---

> _Bhāvataddhitaṃ._

[Thus ends] the `bhāvataddhita`, the abstract suffixes.

---

## Visesataddhita (Suffixes of comparison)

> ‘‘Sabbe ime pāpā ayamimesaṃ visesena pāpo’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `sabbe ime pāpā, ayaṃ imesaṃ visesena pāpo` ("all these are bad; this one among them is bad by distinction") —

---

<a name="r390"></a>

### 390 (§363): Visese taratamisikiyiṭṭhā (`tara`, `tama`, `isika`, `iya`, `iṭṭha` in the sense of distinction) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§363 (390): Visese taratamisikiyiṭṭhā" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m363" description="`tara`, `tama`, `isika`, `iya`, `iṭṭha` in the sense of distinction" />
:::

> Visesatthe tara tama isika iya iṭṭha iccete paccayā honti. Pāpataro, pāpatarā, pāpataraṃ. Tatopi adhiko pāpatamo, pāpatamā, pāpatamaṃ. Pāpisiko, pāpisikā, pāpisikaṃ. Pāpiyo, pāpiyā, pāpiyaṃ. Pāpiṭṭho, pāpiṭṭhā, pāpiṭṭhaṃ. Atisayena pāpiṭṭho, pāpiṭṭhataro. Evaṃ paṭutaro, paṭutamo, paṭisiko, paṭiyo, paṭiṭṭho. Sabbesaṃ atisayena varo varataro, varatamo, varisiko, variyo, variṭṭho. Evaṃ paṇītataro, paṇītatamo.

In the sense of distinction (`visesa`) the suffixes `tara`, `tama`, `isika`, `iya` and `iṭṭha` are used. `pāpataro`, `pāpatarā`, `pāpataraṃ` ("worse"); one who exceeds even that one: `pāpatamo`, `pāpatamā`, `pāpatamaṃ` ("worst"); `pāpisiko`, `pāpisikā`, `pāpisikaṃ`, and `pāpiyo`, `pāpiyā`, `pāpiyaṃ` ("worse"); `pāpiṭṭho`, `pāpiṭṭhā`, `pāpiṭṭhaṃ` ("worst"); exceedingly bad: `pāpiṭṭho`, and `pāpiṭṭhataro` ("worse than the worst"). In the same way `paṭutaro`, `paṭutamo`, `paṭisiko`, `paṭiyo`, `paṭiṭṭho` ("cleverer", "cleverest"); exceedingly excellent among all: `varataro`, `varatamo`, `varisiko`, `variyo`, `variṭṭho` ("more excellent", "most excellent"); and in the same way `paṇītataro`, `paṇītatamo` ("finer", "finest").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | pāpa + si (①⨀) + tara           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pāpa + ~~si~~ + tara            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | pāpatara + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pāpatara + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **pāpataro**                    |      |

|     | pāpa + si (①⨀) + iya            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pāpa + ~~si~~ + iya             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | pāp + (~~a~~ + iya)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | pāpiya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pāpiya + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **pāpiyo**                      |      |

|     | pāpa + si (①⨀) + iṭṭha          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pāpa + ~~si~~ + iṭṭha           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | pāp + (~~a~~ + iṭṭha)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | pāpiṭṭha + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pāpiṭṭha + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **pāpiṭṭho**                    |      |

|     | pāpatara + ā (🚺)               | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pāpatar + (~~a~~ + ā)           | 176 (§237), 67 (§83) |
| →   | pāpatarā + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | pāpatarā + ~~si~~               | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **pāpatarā**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Sabbe ime vuḍḍhā ayamimesaṃ visesena vuḍḍho’’ti atthe iyaiṭṭha ppaccayā honti.

In the sense `sabbe ime vuḍḍhā, ayaṃ imesaṃ visesena vuḍḍho` ("all these are old; this one among them is old by distinction"), the suffixes `iya` and `iṭṭha` are used.

---

<a name="r391"></a>

### 391 (§262): Vuḍḍhassa jo iyiṭṭhesu (`ja` for `vuḍḍha` before `iya`, `iṭṭha`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§262 (391): Vuḍḍhassa jo iyiṭṭhesu" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m262" description="`ja` for `vuḍḍha` before `iya`, `iṭṭha`" />
:::

> Sabbasseva vuḍḍha saddassa jo hoti iya iṭṭha iccetesu paccayesu. Jeyyo, jeṭṭho, ettha ca ‘‘saralopādi’’sutte tu ggahaṇena lopamakatvā ‘‘sarā sare lopa’’nti pubbasare lutte ‘‘kvacāsavaṇṇaṃ lutte’’ti e kāro.

The entire word `vuḍḍha` ("old") becomes `ja` before the suffixes `iya` and `iṭṭha`: `jeyyo` ("older"), `jeṭṭho` ("oldest"). And here, because of the mention of `tu` in the rule [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/1-pathamakanda#m83), that elision is not made; instead, once the preceding vowel has been elided by [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12), the letter `e` is used by [§14 (16): Kvacāsavaṇṇaṃ lutte](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m14).

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | vuḍḍha + si (①⨀) + iya          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vuḍḍha + ~~si~~ + iya           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (vuḍḍha→ja) + iya               | 391 (§262) |
| →   | j + (~~a~~ + iya)               | 13 (§12) |
| →   | j + (i→e) + ya                  | 16 (§14) |
| →   | je + (y)ya                      | 40 (§28) |
| →   | jeyya + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | jeyya + (si→o)                  | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **jeyyo**                       |      |

|     | vuḍḍha + si (①⨀) + iṭṭha        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vuḍḍha + ~~si~~ + iṭṭha         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (vuḍḍha→ja) + iṭṭha             | 391 (§262) |
| →   | j + (~~a~~ + iṭṭha)             | 13 (§12) |
| →   | j + (i→e) + ṭṭha                | 16 (§14) |
| →   | jeṭṭha + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | jeṭṭha + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **jeṭṭho**                      |      |

:::

> ‘‘Iyiṭṭhesū’’ti adhikāro, ‘‘jo’’ti ca vattate.

“`iyiṭṭhesu`” ("before `iya` and `iṭṭha`") is a governing rule ([`adhikāra`](/introduction#types-of-suttas)), and “`jo`” ("becomes `ja`") also continues.

---

<a name="r392"></a>

### 392 (§263): Pasatthassa so ca (`sa`, and `ja`, for `pasattha`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§263 (392): Pasatthassa so ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m263" description="`sa`, and `ja`, for `pasattha`" />
:::

> Sabbasseva pasattha saddassa sā deso hoti, jo ca iyiṭṭhesu. Ayañca pasattho ayañca pasattho sabbe ime pasatthā ayamimesaṃ visesena pasatthoti seyyo, seṭṭho, jeyyo, jeṭṭho.

The entire word `pasattha` ("praised") is replaced by `sa`, and also by `ja`, before `iya` and `iṭṭha`. `ayañca pasattho ayañca pasattho, sabbe ime pasatthā, ayaṃ imesaṃ visesena pasattho` ("this one is praised and this one is praised; all these are praised; this one among them is praised by distinction"): `seyyo` ("better"), `seṭṭho` ("best"), `jeyyo` ("better"), `jeṭṭho` ("best").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | pasattha + si (①⨀) + iya        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pasattha + ~~si~~ + iya         | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (pasattha→sa) + iya             | 392 (§263) |
| →   | s + (~~a~~ + iya)               | 13 (§12) |
| →   | s + (i→e) + ya                  | 16 (§14) |
| →   | se + (y)ya                      | 40 (§28) |
| →   | seyya + si                      | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | seyya + (si→o)                  | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **seyyo**                       |      |

|     | pasattha + si (①⨀) + iṭṭha      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pasattha + ~~si~~ + iṭṭha       | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (pasattha→sa) + iṭṭha           | 392 (§263) |
| →   | s + (~~a~~ + iṭṭha)             | 13 (§12) |
| →   | s + (i→e) + ṭṭha                | 16 (§14) |
| →   | seṭṭha + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | seṭṭha + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **seṭṭho**                      |      |

:::

---

<a name="r393"></a>

### 393 (§264): Antikassa nedo (`neda` for `antika`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§264 (393): Antikassa nedo" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m264" description="`neda` for `antika`" />
:::

> Sabbasseva antika saddassa nedā deso hoti iyiṭṭhesu. Visesena antikoti nediyo, nediṭṭho.

The entire word `antika` ("near") is replaced by `neda` before `iya` and `iṭṭha`. `visesena antiko` ("near by distinction"): `nediyo` ("nearer"), `nediṭṭho` ("nearest").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | antika + si (①⨀) + iya          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | antika + ~~si~~ + iya           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (antika→neda) + iya             | 393 (§264) |
| →   | ned + (~~a~~ + iya)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | nediya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | nediya + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **nediyo**                      |      |

:::

---

<a name="r394"></a>

### 394 (§265): Bāḷhassa sādho (`sādha` for `bāḷha`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§265 (394): Bāḷhassa sādho" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m265" description="`sādha` for `bāḷha`" />
:::

> Sabbasseva bā ḷhasaddassa sādhā deso hoti iyiṭṭhesu. Visesena bāḷhoti sādhiyo, sādhiṭṭho.

The entire word `bāḷha` ("strong") is replaced by `sādha` before `iya` and `iṭṭha`. `visesena bāḷho` ("strong by distinction"): `sādhiyo` ("stronger"), `sādhiṭṭho` ("strongest").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | bāḷha + si (①⨀) + iṭṭha         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bāḷha + ~~si~~ + iṭṭha          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (bāḷha→sādha) + iṭṭha           | 394 (§265) |
| →   | sādh + (~~a~~ + iṭṭha)          | 67 (§83) |
| →   | sādhiṭṭha + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | sādhiṭṭha + (si→o)              | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **sādhiṭṭho**                   |      |

:::

---

<a name="r395"></a>

### 395 (§266): Appassa kaṇa (`kaṇa` for `appa`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§266 (395): Appassa kaṇa" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m266" description="`kaṇa` for `appa`" />
:::

> Sabbassa appa saddassa kaṇa hoti iyiṭṭhesu. Visesena appoti kaṇiyo, kaṇiṭṭho.

The entire word `appa` ("little") becomes `kaṇa` before `iya` and `iṭṭha`. `visesena appo` ("little by distinction"): `kaṇiyo` ("less"), `kaṇiṭṭho` ("least").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | appa + si (①⨀) + iya            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | appa + ~~si~~ + iya             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (appa→kaṇa) + iya               | 395 (§266) |
| →   | kaṇ + (~~a~~ + iya)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | kaṇiya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kaṇiya + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **kaṇiyo**                      |      |

:::

> ‘‘Visesena yuvā’’ti atthe ‘‘kaṇa’’iti vattate.

In the sense `visesena yuvā` ("young by distinction"), “`kaṇa`” continues.

---

<a name="r396"></a>

### 396 (§267): Yuvānañca (And [`kaṇa`] for `yuva`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§267 (396): Yuvānañca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m267" description="And [`kaṇa`] for `yuva`" />
:::

> Sabbassa yuva saddassa kaṇa hoti iyiṭṭhesu. ‘‘Tesu vuddhī’’tiādinā ṇa kārassa na kāro. Kaniyo, kaniṭṭho.

The entire word `yuva` ("young") becomes `kaṇa` before `iya` and `iṭṭha`, and by [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404) and the rest the letter `ṇ` becomes the letter `n`: `kaniyo` ("younger"), `kaniṭṭho` ("youngest").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | yuva + si (①⨀) + iṭṭha          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | yuva + ~~si~~ + iṭṭha           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | (yuva→kaṇa) + iṭṭha             | 396 (§267) |
| →   | ka(ṇ→n)a + iṭṭha                | 370 (§404) |
| →   | kan + (~~a~~ + iṭṭha)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | kaniṭṭha + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kaniṭṭha + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **kaniṭṭho**                    |      |

:::

---

<a name="r397"></a>

### 397 (§268): Vantu mantu vīnañca lopo (`vantu`, `mantu`, `vī` elided before `iya`, `iṭṭha`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§268 (397): Vantu mantu vīnañca lopo" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa/5-pancamakanda#m268" description="`vantu`, `mantu`, `vī` elided before `iya`, `iṭṭha`" />
:::

> Vantumantuvī iccetesaṃ paccayānaṃ lopo hoti iyiṭṭhesu. Sabbe ime guṇavanto ayamimesaṃ visesena guṇavāti guṇiyo, guṇiṭṭho, visesena satimāti satiyo, satiṭṭho, visesena medhāvīti medhiyo, medhiṭṭho iccādi.

The suffixes `vantu`, `mantu` and `vī` are elided before `iya` and `iṭṭha`. `sabbe ime guṇavanto, ayaṃ imesaṃ visesena guṇavā` ("all these are virtuous; this one among them is virtuous by distinction"): `guṇiyo` ("more virtuous"), `guṇiṭṭho` ("most virtuous"); `visesena satimā` ("mindful by distinction"): `satiyo` ("more mindful"), `satiṭṭho` ("most mindful"); `visesena medhāvī` ("wise by distinction"): `medhiyo` ("wiser"), `medhiṭṭho` ("wisest"); and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | guṇavantu + si (①⨀) + iya       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇavantu + ~~si~~ + iya        | 332 (§317) |
| →   | guṇa + (~~vantu~~) + iya        | 397 (§268) |
| →   | guṇ + (~~a~~ + iya)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | guṇiya + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | guṇiya + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **guṇiyo**                      |      |

|     | satimantu + si (①⨀) + iṭṭha     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satimantu + ~~si~~ + iṭṭha      | 332 (§317) |
| →   | sati + (~~mantu~~) + iṭṭha      | 397 (§268) |
| →   | sat + (~~i~~ + iṭṭha)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | satiṭṭha + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | satiṭṭha + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **satiṭṭho**                    |      |

|     | medhāvī + si (①⨀) + iya         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | medhāvī + ~~si~~ + iya          | 332 (§317) |
| →   | medhā + (~~vī~~) + iya          | 397 (§268) |
| →   | medh + (~~ā~~ + iya)            | 67 (§83) |
| →   | medhiya + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | medhiya + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **medhiyo**                     |      |

:::

---

> _Visesataddhitaṃ._

[Thus ends] the `visesataddhita`, the suffixes of comparison.

---

## Assatthitaddhita (Possessive suffixes)

> ‘‘Medhā yassa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatī’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `medhā yassa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("he who has wisdom, or in whom it is found") —

---

<a name="r398"></a>

### 398 (§364): Tadassatthīti vī ca (And `vī` in the sense "he has that") :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§364 (398): Tadassatthīti vī ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m364" description="And `vī` in the sense 'he has that'" />
:::

> Paṭhamāvibhatyantā ‘‘tadassa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati’’ iccetesvatthesu vī paccayo hoti. Medhāmāyā saddehi cāyaṃ. Medhāvī, medhāvino. Itthiyaṃ ī kārantattā ‘‘patibhikkhurājīkārantehi inī’’ti inī, medhāvinī, medhāviniyo. Napuṃsake medhāvi kulaṃ. Evaṃ māyāvī, māyāvinī, māyāvi cittaṃ.

After a word in the first form ① the suffix `vī` is used in the senses "he has that" (`tadassa atthi`) and "it is found in him" (`tasmiṃ vā vijjati`); and this suffix comes after the words `medhā` ("wisdom") and `māyā` ("deceit"). `medhāvī` ("wise"), `medhāvino` ("wise men"). In the feminine, because the word ends in `ī`, `inī` is used by [§240 (193): Pati bhikkhurājīkārantehi inī](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/4-catutthakanda#m240): `medhāvinī` ("a wise woman"), `medhāviniyo` ("wise women"). In the neuter, `medhāvi kulaṃ` ("a wise family"). In the same way `māyāvī` ("deceitful"), `māyāvinī` ("a deceitful woman"), `māyāvi cittaṃ` ("a deceitful mind").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | medhā + si (①⨀) + vī            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | medhā + ~~si~~ + vī             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | medhāvī + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | medhāvī + ~~si~~ (🚹①⨀)         | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **medhāvī**                     |      |

|     | medhāvī (🚹①⨂)                  | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | medhāv(ī→i)                     | 144 (§84) |
| →   | medhāvi + yo                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | medhāvi + (yo→no)               | 151 (§225) |
| →   | **medhāvino**                   |      |

|     | medhāvī + inī (🚺)              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | medhāv + (~~ī~~ + inī)          | 193 (§240), 67 (§83) |
| →   | medhāvinī + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | medhāvinī + ~~si~~              | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **medhāvinī**                   |      |

|     | medhāvī (🚻①⨀)                  | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | medhāv(ī→i)                     | 144 (§84) |
| →   | medhāvi + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | medhāvi + ~~si~~                | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **medhāvi**                     |      |

:::

> Ca ggahaṇena so i lava ālā dippaccayā ca. Yathā – sumedhā yassa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatīti sumedhaso, rassattaṃ. Evaṃ lomaso. Picchaṃ assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatīti picchilo. Evaṃ phenilo, tuṇḍilo, jaṭilo. Kesā assa atthīti kesavo, vācālo iccādi.

By the mention of `ca`, the suffixes `sa`, `ila`, `va`, `āla` and the rest are used as well. For example, `sumedhā yassa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("he who has great wisdom, or in whom it is found"): `sumedhaso` ("very wise"), with shortening; in the same way `lomaso` ("hairy"). `picchaṃ assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("it has gum, or gum is found in it"): `picchilo` ("slimy"); in the same way `phenilo` ("having foam"), `tuṇḍilo` ("having a beak"), `jaṭilo` ("wearing matted hair"). `kesā assa atthi` ("he has hair"): `kesavo` ("having hair"); `vācālo` ("talkative"); and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | sumedhā + si (①⨀) + sa          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sumedhā + ~~si~~ + sa           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | sumedh(ā→a)sa                   | 354 (§403) |
| →   | sumedhasa + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | sumedhasa + (si→o)              | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **sumedhaso**                   |      |

|     | piccha + si (①⨀) + ila          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | piccha + ~~si~~ + ila           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | picch + (~~a~~ + ila)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | picchila + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | picchila + (si→o)               | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **picchilo**                    |      |

|     | vācā + si (①⨀) + āla            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vācā + ~~si~~ + āla             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | vāc + (~~ā~~ + āla)             | 67 (§83) |
| →   | vācāla + si                     | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | vācāla + (si→o)                 | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **vācālo**                      |      |

:::

> ‘‘Tapo assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatī’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `tapo assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("he has austerity, or it is found in him") —

> ‘‘Tadassatthī’’ti adhikāro.

“`tadassatthi`” ("he has that") is a governing rule ([`adhikāra`](/introduction#types-of-suttas)).

---

<a name="r399"></a>

### 399 (§365): Tapādito sī (`sī` after `tapa` and the like: "having austerity") :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§365 (399): Tapādito sī" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m365" description="`sī` after `tapa` and the like: 'having austerity'" />
:::

> Tapa iccevamādito sī paccayo hoti ‘‘tadassatthi’’ iccetasmiṃ atthe. Sassa dvibhāvo. Tapassī, tapassino, tapassinī, tapassi. Evaṃ tejassī, yasassī, manassī, payassī.

After `tapa` ("austerity") and the like the suffix `sī` is used in the sense "he has that", and the `s` is doubled: `tapassī` ("an ascetic"), `tapassino` ("ascetics"), `tapassinī` ("a female ascetic"), `tapassi` ("ascetic", neuter). In the same way `tejassī` ("splendid"), `yasassī` ("famous"), `manassī` ("intelligent"), `payassī` ("rich in milk").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | tapa + si (①⨀) + sī             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tapa + ~~si~~ + sī              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | tapa + (s)sī                    | 40 (§28) |
| →   | tapassī + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | tapassī + ~~si~~                | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **tapassī**                     |      |

|     | tapassī + inī (🚺)              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tapass + (~~ī~~ + inī)          | 193 (§240), 67 (§83) |
| →   | tapassinī + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | tapassinī + ~~si~~              | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **tapassinī**                   |      |

:::

> ‘‘Daṇḍo assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatī’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `daṇḍo assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("he has a staff, or it is found on him") —

---

<a name="r400"></a>

### 400 (§366): Daṇḍādito ikaī (`ika`, `ī` after `daṇḍa` and the like: "having a staff") :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§366 (400): Daṇḍādito ikaī" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m366" description="`ika`, `ī` after `daṇḍa` and the like: 'having a staff'" />
:::

> Ādi saddoyaṃ pakārattho, daṇḍa iccevamādito avaṇṇantā ika ī iccete paccayā honti ‘‘tadassatthi’’ iccetasmiṃ atthe. Daṇḍiko, daṇḍī, daṇḍino, daṇḍinī. Evaṃ māliko, mālī, mālinī, chattiko, chattī, rūpiko, rūpī, kesiko, kesī, saṅghī, ñāṇī, hatthī iccādi.

Here the word `ādi` ("and the like") has the sense of "kind" (`pakāra`): after `daṇḍa` ("a staff") and words of that kind, ending in `a` or `ā`, the suffixes `ika` and `ī` are used in the sense "he has that". `daṇḍiko`, `daṇḍī` ("a staff-bearer"), `daṇḍino` ("staff-bearers"), `daṇḍinī` ("a woman with a staff"). In the same way `māliko`, `mālī` ("garlanded"), `mālinī` ("a garlanded woman"); `chattiko`, `chattī` ("carrying a parasol"); `rūpiko`, `rūpī` ("having form"); `kesiko`, `kesī` ("having hair"); `saṅghī` ("having a following"); `ñāṇī` ("having knowledge"); `hatthī` ("an elephant"); and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | daṇḍa + si (①⨀) + ika           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | daṇḍa + ~~si~~ + ika            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | daṇḍ + (~~a~~ + ika)            | 67 (§83) |
| →   | daṇḍika + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | daṇḍika + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **daṇḍiko**                     |      |

|     | daṇḍa + si (①⨀) + ī             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | daṇḍa + ~~si~~ + ī              | 332 (§317) |
| →   | daṇḍ + (~~a~~ + ī)              | 67 (§83) |
| →   | daṇḍī + si (①⨀)                | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | daṇḍī + ~~si~~                  | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **daṇḍī**                       |      |

|     | mālā + si (①⨀) + ī              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mālā + ~~si~~ + ī               | 332 (§317) |
| →   | māl + (~~ā~~ + ī)               | 67 (§83) |
| →   | mālī + si (①⨀)                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | mālī + ~~si~~                   | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **mālī**                        |      |

:::

---

<a name="r401"></a>

### 401 (§367): Madhvādito ro (`ra` after `madhu` and the like: "having sweetness") :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§367 (401): Madhvādito ro" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m367" description="`ra` after `madhu` and the like: 'having sweetness'" />
:::

> Madhu ādito ra ppaccayo hoti ‘‘tadassatthī’’ti atthe. Madhu assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatīti madhuro guḷo, madhurā sakkharā, madhuraṃ khīraṃ, kuñjā hanū etassa santīti kuñjaro, sabbasmiṃ vattabbe mukhamassa atthīti mukharo, susi assa atthīti susiro. Evaṃ ruciro, nagaro.

After `madhu` ("honey") and the like the suffix `ra` is used in the sense "he has that". `madhu assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("it has honey, or honey is found in it"): `madhuro guḷo` ("sweet molasses"), `madhurā sakkharā` ("sweet sugar"), `madhuraṃ khīraṃ` ("sweet milk"); `kuñjā hanū etassa santi` ("its jaws are `kuñjā`"): `kuñjaro` ("an elephant"); `sabbasmiṃ vattabbe mukhaṃ assa atthi` ("he has a mouth for everything that can be said"): `mukharo` ("talkative"); `susi assa atthi` ("it has a hollow"): `susiro` ("hollow"). In the same way `ruciro` ("beautiful"), `nagaro` ("a town").

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | madhu + si (①⨀) + ra            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | madhu + ~~si~~ + ra             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | madhura + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | madhura + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **madhuro**                     |      |

|     | madhura + ā (🚺)                | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | madhur + (~~a~~ + ā)            | 176 (§237), 67 (§83) |
| →   | madhurā + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | madhurā + ~~si~~                | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **madhurā**                     |      |

|     | kuñjā + si (①⨀) + ra            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kuñjā + ~~si~~ + ra             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | kuñj(ā→a)ra                     | 354 (§403) |
| →   | kuñjara + si                    | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | kuñjara + (si→o)                | 66 (§104) |
| →   | **kuñjaro**                     |      |

:::

> ‘‘Guṇo assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatī’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `guṇo assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("he has virtue, or it is found in him") —

---

<a name="r402"></a>

### 402 (§368): Guṇāditovantu (`vantu` after `guṇa` and the like: "having virtue") :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§368 (402): Guṇādito vantu" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m368" description="`vantu` after `guṇa` and the like: 'having virtue'" />
:::

> Guṇa iccevamādito vantu ppaccayo hoti ‘‘tadassa atthī’’ti atthe. Vibhattilope, nāmabyapadese ca kate syā dyuppatti. Guṇavantu si, ‘‘savibhattissa, ntussā’’ti adhikicca ‘‘ā simhī’’ti ā ttaṃ, guṇavā puriso, sesaṃ ñeyyaṃ. Evaṃ gaṇavā, kulavā iccādayo. Itthiyaṃ ‘‘ṇava ṇika ṇeyyaṇantūhī’’ti īpaccayo, ‘‘vā’’ti vattamāne ‘‘ntussa tamīkāre’’ti ta kāro, guṇavatī, guṇavantī iccādi. Napuṃsake ‘‘aṃ napuṃsake’’ti savibhattissa ntussa a mādeso, guṇavaṃ iccādi.

After `guṇa` ("virtue") and the like the suffix `vantu` is used in the sense "he has that". Once the inflection has been elided and the word designated a noun, `si` and the rest arise. In `guṇavantu` + `si`, with “`savibhattissa`” ([§120 (243): Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/2-dutiyakanda#m120)) and “`ntussa`” ([§122 (99): Ntussa nto](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/2-dutiyakanda#m122)) governing, the change to `ā` is made by [§124 (98): Ā simhi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/2-dutiyakanda#m124): `guṇavā puriso` ("a virtuous man"); the rest should be understood. In the same way `gaṇavā` ("having a following"), `kulavā` ("of good family"), and so on. In the feminine, the suffix `ī` is used by [§239 (190): Ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntuhi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/4-catutthakanda#m239), and, while “`vā`” continues, the letter `ta` by [§241 (191): Ntussa tamīkāre](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/4-catutthakanda#m241): `guṇavatī`, `guṇavantī` ("a virtuous woman"), and so on. In the neuter, by [§125 (198): Aṃ napuṃsake](/kaccayana/2-namakappa/2-dutiyakanda#m125) `ntu` together with its inflection is replaced by `aṃ`: `guṇavaṃ`, and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | guṇa + si (①⨀) + vantu          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇa + ~~si~~ + vantu           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | guṇavantu + si (①⨀)            | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + si→ā)           | 98 (§124) |
| →   | **guṇavā**                      |      |

|     | guṇavantu + ī (🚺)              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu→ta) + ī           | 190 (§239), 191 (§241) |
| →   | guṇavat + (~~a~~ + ī)           | 67 (§83) |
| →   | guṇavatī + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | guṇavatī + ~~si~~               | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **guṇavatī**                    |      |

|     | guṇavantu + ī (🚺)              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇavant + (~~u~~ + ī)          | 190 (§239), 67 (§83) |
| →   | guṇavantī + si                  | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | guṇavantī + ~~si~~              | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **guṇavantī**                   |      |

|     | guṇavantu + si (🚻①⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + si → aṃ)        | 198 (§125) |
| →   | **guṇavaṃ**                     |      |

:::

> ‘‘Sati assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjatī’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `sati assa atthi, tasmiṃ vā vijjati` ("he has mindfulness, or it is found in him") —

> ‘‘Tadassatthī’’ti vattate.

“`tadassatthi`” ("he has that") continues.

---

<a name="r403"></a>

### 403 (§369): Satyādīhi mantu (`mantu` after `sati` and the like: "having mindfulness") :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§369 (403): Satyādīhi mantu" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m369" description="`mantu` after `sati` and the like: 'having mindfulness'" />
:::

> Sati iccevamādīhi avaṇṇantarahitehi paṭhamāvibhatyantehi liṅgehi mantu ppaccayo hoti ‘‘tadassatthī’’ti atthe. Sesaṃ guṇava ntusamaṃ. Satimā, satimatī, satimantī, satimaṃ. Evaṃ dhitimā, gatimā iccādayo.

After `sati` ("mindfulness") and the like, lemmas in the first form ① that do not end in `a` or `ā`, the suffix `mantu` is used in the sense "he has that"; the rest is the same as for `guṇavantu`. `satimā` ("mindful"), `satimatī`, `satimantī` ("a mindful woman"), `satimaṃ` ("mindful", neuter). In the same way `dhitimā` ("resolute"), `gatimā` ("retentive"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | sati + si (①⨀) + mantu          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sati + ~~si~~ + mantu           | 332 (§317) |
| →   | satimantu + si (🚹①⨀)           | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | satima + (ntu + si → ā)         | 98 (§124) |
| →   | **satimā**                      |      |

|     | satimantu + ī (🚺)              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satima + (ntu→ta) + ī           | 190 (§239), 191 (§241) |
| →   | satimatī + si                   | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | satimatī + ~~si~~               | 74 (§220) |
| →   | **satimatī**                    |      |

:::

> Tathā ‘‘āyu assa atthīti āyu mantu’’ iccatra –

Likewise, in `āyu assa atthi` ("he has life"), `āyu` + `mantu`, here —

---

<a name="r404"></a>

### 404 (§371): Āyussukārāsamantumhi (`asa` for the `u` of `āyu` before `mantu`: `āyasmā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§371 (404): Āyussukārāsa mantumhi" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m371" description="`asa` for the `u` of `āyu` before `mantu`: `āyasmā`" />
:::

> Āyussa ukāro asa hoti mantu mhīti asādeso. Āyasmā, sesaṃ samaṃ. Gāvo assa santīti gomā, gomanto, gomatī, gomantī, gomaṃ kulaṃ iccādi.

The letter `u` of `āyu` ("life") becomes `asa` before `mantu`: hence the replacement `asa`. `āyasmā` ("venerable"); the rest is the same. `gāvo assa santi` ("he has cattle"): `gomā` ("an owner of cattle"), `gomanto` ("owners of cattle"), `gomatī`, `gomantī` ("a woman who owns cattle"), `gomaṃ kulaṃ` ("a cattle-owning family"), and so on.

:::tip[Derivation]

|     | āyu + si (①⨀) + mantu           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | āyu + ~~si~~ + mantu            | 332 (§317) |
| →   | āy(u→asa) + mantu               | 404 (§371) |
| →   | āyasmantu + si (🚹①⨀)           | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | āyasma + (ntu + si → ā)         | 98 (§124) |
| →   | **āyasmā**                      |      |

|     | go + yo (①⨂) + mantu            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | go + ~~yo~~ + mantu             | 332 (§317) |
| →   | gomantu + si (🚹①⨀)             | 334 (§601), 63 (§55) |
| →   | goma + (ntu + si → ā)           | 98 (§124) |
| →   | **gomā**                        |      |

|     | gomantu + yo (🚹①⨂)             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | goma + (ntu + yo → nto)         | 99 (§122) |
| →   | **gomanto**                     |      |

:::

> ‘‘Saddhā assa atthī’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `saddhā assa atthi` ("he has faith") —

---

<a name="r405"></a>
